## st-0235 — The Railway Children (tek epizod)
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
The young child live in a big house. The house is near a train place. Every day, they walk to the line. They love watching the big train pass. The man and girl look at people. People look back with big eyes. The child love the train very much. They look every day. The train is very big. The people look out. Everyone feel good. The day is long. The sun is high.

One day, something is very bad. A bad thing cause ground to fall. The ground is on the line. A train come around the turn. It might hit the ground. People could die in a bad event. The child know they must take action. They run fast now. They see the problem right away. The train come fast. The child know they must help everyone. They look around for something good. They need to stop the train.

The child find something black. They run down the way now. They hear the train come. Both child wave the black thing high. The driver see the color. He stop the big train now. The train stop near the fallen ground. The driver step down. He asks what happen. He thank the child for helping everyone. People look out and make sound. They make sound for the child. The child feel very good.

Later that week, the company send a letter. People invite the young child. They give them a good reward. Everyone in town talk about the child. The family be very good. Local people talk about the event. The child feel very proud. The family live in the house. They look at the train again. The train pass every day. Everyone be safe now. The story end well.

### A2
The young children lived in a big house. The house was near a station. Every day, they walked down to the line. They loved watching the huge trains pass by. The brother and sister always stood there. They watched people inside the cars. Soon, the friendly people began looking back. They started seeing the kids every day. The children felt very happy then. They always smiled at the people. Life was good for the family.

One afternoon, the children noticed a problem. There was a bad issue on the line. A long water fall had caused rocks to fall. The rocks were right across the tracks. A fast train would hit the rocks. Many people might die in a bad car event. They knew action was necessary now. The kids found some red clothes. They ran as fast as they could. They ran down the line to the station. They wanted to stop any problem very soon.

They heard the sound of a train. Both children held the red clothes high. They moved the red color in the air. The driver saw the red color well. He stopped the big train quickly. The train stopped near the fallen rocks. The driver stepped down from the car. He asked what had happened here. He thanked the kids for saving everyone. The people looked through their windows. They were well because of the young kids.

The people called for the little kids. The brother and sister were good helpers. Later that week, the company sent a letter. The letter was special for the family. The managers called the young kids. They went to the station again. The company gave them a good thing. Everyone in the town talked about them. The family was very happy. From that day, life was different. They would always remember this time.

### B1
The young children lived in a big house near a town station. Every day, they walked down to the metal tracks. They loved watching the huge machines pass by. The brother and sister always waved happily at the people inside the windows. Soon, the friendly visitors began waving back at them with big smiles. They have lived there for many years now. They always look forward to seeing the morning vehicles roll along the path. If the weather is good, they spend hours outside watching every single journey. The local people know the family very well. The children always feel happy when they see friendly faces looking out at them.

One afternoon, the kids noticed that something was wrong on the line. A heavy storm had caused stones to fall across the tracks. This happened just before a sharp turn. If a fast vehicle came around that corner, it would crash into the stones. Many people might die in such a bad accident. They knew that immediate action was necessary to prevent a major problem. If they do nothing, a terrible event will happen very soon. The situation is extremely dangerous for everyone on the transport. They have already understood that they must act right now. They want to save the passengers from any trouble.

The good kids quickly found some bright red clothes in their home. They ran as fast as they could down the path toward the danger area. When they heard the high engine approach, both children waved the red clothes high. They held the clothes up in the air. The driver saw the bright warning color and immediately stopped the heavy machine. He applied the stop mechanism. The children have acted with great bravery today. If they had not run down the path, the vehicle would have crashed into the stones. They have shown how bright they really are. The driver feels very grateful for their quick help.

The massive train stopped safely only a short distance away from the fallen stones. The grateful driver stepped down from the engine and asked what had happened. When he saw the dangerous stones blocking the path, he thanked the kids. He thanked them for saving everyone. The passengers looked out through their windows and called out for the bright brother and sister. They have never seen such young champions before. If the children had stayed home, the story would have ended in bad trouble. Everyone is now talking about the wonderful action of the kids. The passengers feel safe because the children stopped the vehicle. They are happy to be alive after such a close moment.

Later that week, the company sent a special letter to the house. The managers invited the young individuals to the station to give them a wonderful reward. Everyone in the town talked about how the children had saved the passengers from danger. From that day forward, the family was celebrated by all the local people. The company has also offered them a free trip on the path. If they visit the station again, the managers will give them another big gift. The family has kept the special letter in a safe place inside their home. They have shown that even young people can make a huge difference in the world. The town will always remember this amazing event. The children are very proud of what they have achieved.

### B2
The young children lived in a big house near a station. Every day, they walked down to the metal tracks because they loved watching the huge trains pass by. The brother and sister always waved happily at the people inside the windows. Soon, the friendly people began waving back at them with big smiles. The family lived near the rail line for many years. Every morning, the children looked out of their windows to watch the massive vehicles move along the iron path. They knew each schedule by heart because the big machines were very loud. Sometimes, the children talked about how wonderful it would be to ride inside one of those long machines. They often played games near the station while their mother stayed inside the house. The local community was small, and everyone knew the family very well. The children always felt safe when they played near the open field. Their parents told them to stay away from the active rail line during the late afternoon. However, the children loved the sound of the heavy engines too much to stay completely away from the path. They liked to wave at the passengers who looked out through the glass. Each passenger usually smiled and waved back, which made the children feel very happy. This simple daily routine continued for a long time until one afternoon changed everything.

One afternoon, the children noticed that something was wrong on the line. A heavy storm had caused rocks to fall across the tracks just before a sharp turn. If a fast train came around that corner, it would crash into the stones and many people might die. They knew that immediate action was necessary to prevent a disaster. The sky had grown dark very quickly while the storm brought heavy wind and rain. Water covered parts of the ground near the station. When the rain finally stopped, the children walked down to the track to check the area. They saw that a large section of stone had fallen from the hill above. The heavy rocks blocked the metal path completely. The children understood that an oncoming train would not see the danger until it was too late. If the driver did not stop the engine immediately, a terrible accident would happen. Many innocent passengers might lose their lives if nobody warned the driver. They felt a deep sense of responsibility because they were the only ones who knew about the problem. Without any hesitation, the children decided that they had to find a way to stop the vehicle before it reached the curve. Every second counted now, and they had to act as quickly as possible to save everyone.

The kids quickly found some bright red clothes in their home. They ran as fast as they could down the path toward the danger area. When they heard the loud engine approach, both children waved the red clothes high in the air. The smart driver saw the bright warning color and immediately applied the heavy brakes. They ran back into the house and searched through every room until they found the bright garments. The clothes were old, but the color was very strong and easy to see from a long distance. They rushed back outside and ran as fast as they could along the side of the track. The ground was still wet from the storm, but they did not care about the mud. They reached the curve just before the scheduled time of the afternoon train. Soon, they heard the loud sound of the engine coming around the bend. Both children stood right in the middle of the path and held the red clothes high above their heads. They shook the material back and forth so the driver would notice them. The driver looked out through the front window and saw the bright warning color immediately. He realized that something was wrong and pulled the heavy brakes with all his power. The massive machine began to slow down while sparks flew from the metal wheels.

The massive train stopped safely only a few meters away from the fallen rocks. The grateful driver stepped down from the engine and asked what had happened. When he saw the dangerous stones blocking the path, he thanked the kids for saving everyone. The passengers looked out through their windows and cheered loudly for the brother and sister. The heavy engine came to a complete stop just a few meters away from the pile of stone. If the driver had applied the brakes even a second later, the train would have crashed directly into the rocks. The passengers inside the carriages fell forward from the sudden stop, but nobody was hurt. The driver opened the door and stepped down onto the ground with a shocked expression on his face. He walked toward the children and asked what had caused them to wave the red clothes. When he saw the massive stones blocking the line, he understood the situation completely. He looked at the young kids with deep respect and thanked them for saving the lives of everyone on board. The passengers opened the windows to see what was happening outside. When they saw the fallen rocks and understood the danger, they began to shout and clap for the brother and sister. Everyone agreed that the children had shown incredible intelligence and courage.

Later that week, the company sent a special letter to the house. The managers invited the young heroes to the station to give them a wonderful reward. Everyone in the town talked about how the children had saved the passengers from danger. From that day forward, the family was celebrated by all the local people. The local newspaper published a long article about the event to honor the kids. Several days later, an official letter arrived in the mail for the family. The managers of the railway company wrote that the children were true heroes. They invited the brother and sister to visit the main station for a special ceremony. During the event, the company gave them a wonderful reward and a large cake. Many people from the town came to the station to watch the celebration and take pictures. The parents stood nearby with big smiles, feeling very proud of their children. From that moment on, the family was known throughout the entire region. Every person in the community remembered the day when the two young kids saved the train from disaster. The children continued to live near the station, but they never forgot the lessons of that extraordinary week.

### C1
Nestled quietly against a sprawling, sunlit meadow, the grand old house stood as a silent witness to the daily rhythm of the railway. Within its walls lived the children, whose days were measured not by clocks, but by the rhythmic chug and heavy sigh of passing steam engines. Every morning, they walked down the grassy slope toward the gleaming metal tracks to watch the magnificent iron beasts roar past. What began as a distant fascination soon blossomed into a warm, unspoken bond between the young watchers and the travelers behind the glass. The brother and sister would wave their hands with bright enthusiasm, and invariably, a friendly face would smile and wave back from a compartment window. For many years, the family made this picturesque landscape their home, finding comfort in the predictable choreography of the rails. The children knew each timetable by heart, recognizing the pitch and volume of every engine as it conquered the incline. Often, during their quiet games near the station, they would whisper to one another about the magic of riding inside such a long machine. While their mother remained occupied within the house, the siblings roamed freely through the small, tightly knit community where everyone knew their names. They felt entirely secure within the embrace of the open fields and the friendly local folk who populated the valley. Yet, their parents frequently warned them to keep a safe distance from the active rail line during the unpredictable late afternoon hours. Drawn irresistibly by the thunderous music of the heavy engines, the children could never quite tear themselves away from the edge of the path. Each reciprocal wave from a passing passenger reinforced their affection for the place, anchoring them to a simple routine that seemed destined to last forever.

That fragile certainty shattered on a Tuesday afternoon when the familiar atmosphere of the valley turned abruptly heavy and oppressive. A violent storm had swept across the hills with startling speed, unleashing torrential rain and howling winds that battered the countryside. Water rushed in muddy torrents across the gravel, transforming the usually tranquil station grounds into a temporary lake. When the tempest finally exhausted itself and the sky cleared, the children instinctively walked down to inspect the tracks. What they discovered froze them in their tracks with a sudden, icy dread. A massive section of the hillside had given way under the weight of the water, sending tons of jagged rock tumbling down to bury the iron path completely. The children stared at the barrier of stone, their young minds racing to calculate the terrifying implications of the slide. They knew that the scheduled afternoon express was due to round the sharp curve shortly, moving at a speed that left no room for hesitation. If the driver failed to spot the obstruction in time, the heavy engine would plow directly into the boulders with catastrophic results. Many innocent lives hung in the balance, and the realization settled heavily upon their shoulders like a physical weight. Not a single adult was in sight, leaving them as the sole guardians of an impending disaster. Without exchanging a single word of doubt, they understood that they alone bore the responsibility of stopping the train before it reached the bend. Every passing second ticked away like a countdown, demanding immediate, desperate action to avert tragedy.

Acting with frantic urgency, the brother and sister dashed back up the hill and tore through the house in search of something bright enough to catch a distant eye. In the back of a wardrobe, they finally discovered several old, discarded garments of a striking, vibrant red. Though faded by years of wear, the fabric retained a fierce intensity that would surely pierce through the hazy afternoon light. Clutching the garments tightly against their chests, they bolted back outside and sprinted down the slippery path toward the danger zone. The ground remained treacherous with deep mud and standing water, but neither child slowed their pace for a single instant. They reached the sharp curve just minutes before the scheduled arrival of the heavy express train. Soon, the familiar, deep-throated roar of the engine echoed through the valley, announcing the rapid approach of the massive machine. Both children scrambled directly onto the center of the tracks, holding the red garments high above their heads with trembling arms. They shook the fabric back and forth in a frantic arc, pouring all their remaining energy into the desperate signal. High up in the cab, the observant driver caught the brilliant flash of red against the dull green background of the hillside. Instantly recognizing the universal color of alarm, he slammed his hands onto the heavy brakes with all his might. The colossal iron engine shrieked in protest, sending a brilliant cascade of sparks flying from the locked metal wheels as it fought against its own momentum.

Miraculously, the towering machine ground to a halt merely a few meters away from the towering pile of jagged rocks. The silence that followed was heavy, broken only by the hissing steam and the pounding hearts of the two young saviors. Looking down from the cab, the driver wiped the sweat from his forehead and stepped slowly down onto the gravel path. When he traced the line of sight to the massive stones blocking the rails, the color drained entirely from his face. He looked at the children with a mixture of profound shock and immense gratitude, realizing that their quick thinking had averted a massacre. Inside the carriages, passengers began pressing against the glass, murmuring anxiously as they tried to understand the sudden stop. The driver raised his voice to explain the situation to the bewildered travelers, pointing toward the fallen boulders just ahead of the engine. Upon grasping the sheer magnitude of the averted danger, the passengers opened their windows and erupted into thunderous cheers for the brother and sister. People shouted words of praise and clapped with such fervor that the sound echoed off the hillsides. Everyone agreed that the children had displayed a level of courage and intelligence far beyond their years. The driver knelt beside them, shaking their small hands and promising that the railway company would hear of their heroic deed.

True to his word, an official letter arrived at the family's doorstep only a few days later, bearing the stamp of the regional railway company. The managers wrote glowingly of the children's bravery, officially declaring them the saviors of the afternoon express. An invitation was extended for the brother and sister to visit the main station for a special public ceremony in their honor. When the day arrived, townsfolk gathered in large numbers to witness the celebration, bringing gifts, a massive decorated cake, and loud applause. Proudly standing nearby, the parents beamed with a deep, quiet joy as their children received formal recognition for their extraordinary deeds. From that remarkable afternoon onward, the family's name became synonymous with courage throughout the entire region. The local newspaper printed a lengthy, detailed account of the event, ensuring that the story would pass into local legend. Although the children continued to live peacefully by the station and watch the trains roll past, their perspective had fundamentally shifted. They no longer viewed the iron tracks merely as a source of afternoon entertainment, but as the stage where they had proven their mettle. The memory of that turbulent week remained etched in their hearts, reminding them forever of the profound difference ordinary people can make when courage calls.

## st-0236 — The Book of Dragons: The Book of Beasts
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, a young child finds an old book. The book is in a room. The room is very still. He opens the cover. Many things happen now. Things move very fast. He sees small animals. Every thing goes into the book. Night comes to the house. The house is very still. The book close. The book is closed.

The child sits down. He has a good face. He does a good job. He keeps the book in a place. He goes to rest now. He has good thoughts. The night is long. He stays until morning comes. He is a good person. The day is over now. He rests well in the night.

He decided to keep the book in a safe place so that no other trouble would happen. That classic day ended well, and he went to sleep with sweet dreams of magic adventures. He felt proud of himself because he had solved the problem without any help from adults. The room was completely clean again, and there was no trace of the strange events left behind. He placed the heavy book on the highest shelf where it could not be reached by anyone else. Sleep soon came over him as he thought about the amazing experiences of the afternoon. He closed his eyes and let his body rest after such a long and tiring day. The house was dark and peaceful, with no sound except for the wind outside the window. He knew that tomorrow would bring new stories and regular lessons at school. However, he would always remember the day when the book came alive in his hands. It was an experience that no other person in the town could ever imagine. He felt grateful for his safety and the successful end of the whole event. He woke up feeling happy and ready to start a brand new day of life. The book stayed high upon the shelf, resting quietly until another time.

### C1
Deep within a forgotten chamber of his ancestral home, a young boy stumbled upon a volume whose very presence commanded reverence. The tome was bound in darkened, weathered leather, embossed with cryptic symbols that seemed to shift slightly under his gaze. As his fingers brushed the heavy cover, a sudden wave of arcane energy rippled through the quiet room. Every detail of the dusty parlor began to warp as the mysterious force grew stronger with each passing second. He wondered what would happen if he let these unbridled currents continue without intervention. The entire space filled with a brilliant, luminescent glow that radiated directly from the ancient pages.

He understood immediately that he must pay meticulous attention to the unfolding chaos around him. There was precious little time to lose if he wished to restore order and keep the household safe. A multitude of bizarre entities materialized from thin air, darting about while he stood frozen in the center of the room. He reflected on the distant past, pondering how such a formidable object could have rested quietly for decades. The massive book had sat untouched upon a wooden table where nobody dared to disturb its slumber. Yet now, the pages had turned of their own accord, unleashing a potent and uncontrollable draft.

The young child recognized that he was facing a severe situation requiring swift and decisive action. Taking a deep, stabilizing breath, he braced himself for whatever new anomaly might manifest from the ether. An incandescent brilliance poured from the binding, saturating every corner of the expansive chamber in blinding white. He walked cautiously toward the central table, hoping to force the stubborn, snapping covers shut once more. Alas, the magical surge proved far too potent, and a cascade of bewildering phenomena erupted instantly. He watched helplessly as the very geometry of the room warped and shifted before his disbelieving eyes.

It was a fleeting moment of profound doubt, but he resolved to maintain his composure against the rising tide. Without warning, extraordinary creatures leaped forth from the open text, scattering wildly across the domestic landscape. Miniscule winged beasts and other fantastical fauna suddenly sprang into corporeal existence within the mundane world. The boy understood that he alone was responsible for recapturing these liberated entities before catastrophe struck. Certain animals scrambled desperately for cover, while others bounded audaciously across tables and upholstered chairs. Though the task appeared overwhelmingly difficult, a strange calmness settled over his racing spirit.

He attempted to corner each elusive creature with meticulous care so that the house might return to normalcy. Had he hesitated for even a moment, the rampaging beasts might have wrought irreversible destruction. The creatures moved with such astonishing velocity that casual observers from outside would have deemed them mere blurs. He snatched a discarded fabric cloth from a nearby chair, intending to use it as an improvised net. Following brief, frantic pursuits through the living room and adjoining kitchen, he secured his initial targets. The liberated animals dashed everywhere, filling the halls with shrill cries that echoed against the plaster walls.

He sprinted after a particularly small beast that sought refuge behind the heavy velvet sofa in the far corner. With a sudden, coordinated lunge of his hands, he managed to pin the wriggling creature securely. Another mischievous animal bounded upward onto a towering bookshelf, dislodging several delicate ornaments in its wake. He was forced to scramble awkwardly up the wooden shelves to reach the topmost tier where the beast perched. With every successful capture, a surge of quiet confidence bolstered his resolve to finish the ordeal. The room remained a chaotic blur of motion and sound, yet he refused to abandon his daunting mission.

He knew full well that failure would leave his home in permanent disarray, buried beneath layers of impossible magic. Driven by this stark realization, he exerted every ounce of his stamina to herd the remaining creatures. The exhausting chase persisted for hours until the vast majority of the escapees were cornered together. Relying on his native ingenuity and steadfast courage, he began guiding the bewildered beasts back toward the book. One by one, every last stray entity was coaxed safely back between the ancient, yellowed leaves. As twilight approached, the chaotic energies subsided and the heavy volume slammed shut on its own accord.

Collapsing onto a nearby chair with a victorious smile, the boy savored the sweet taste of hard-won triumph. He resolved to conceal the dangerous artifact in a secure location where no further accidents could occur. That remarkable day drew to a peaceful close, leaving him ready to drift into dreams of grand adventures. A profound sense of pride warmed his chest because he had resolved the crisis entirely on his own. The parlor was spotless once more, bearing no physical evidence of the surreal events that had transpired. He hoisted the heavy tome into his arms, carrying it toward the highest shelf in the darkened study.

Placing the volume safely out of reach, he ensured that no curious hands would disturb its slumber again. Exhaustion finally overtook his limbs as he reflected on the extraordinary occurrences of the afternoon. He closed his eyes, allowing his weary body to sink deep into the mattress for a well-deserved rest. The house stood dark and tranquil, undisturbed by any sound save for the autumn wind rustling outside. He understood that tomorrow would bring ordinary school lessons and the predictable rhythm of daily life. Yet he knew he would forever cherish the momentous afternoon when literature literally came alive in his hands.

It remained an experience so fantastical that no other resident of the quiet town could ever truly fathom it. A deep sense of gratitude for his safety and success washed over him as he drifted closer to sleep. The chamber stayed wonderfully quiet and orderly while he slumbered peacefully through the deep hours of night. When dawn eventually broke, golden rays of sunlight streamed warmly through the glass windowpanes. He awoke refreshed and eager to embrace whatever ordinary wonders the new day might present. High upon the shelf, the leather-bound book rested in absolute silence, waiting patiently for another age.

### A1

The man runs toward them. He sees the friendly face. He stops and stands still. He falls down on his legs. He talks about his solitary life. His sound is hard now. He gives help to the group.

## st-0238 — Wind in the Willows: The Wild Wood (epizod)
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Kostebek want see big world. He walks far from small home. He reaches place. Place be Wild Forest. Nice things grow in place. Wind make sound. Wind make different sound. Night come. Night make him feel little. He stays under plant part. Things move on land. Every sound make body move fast. He be by himself. He stays under plant base.

Good friend start look for him. They call name in forest. They walk between old things with light. Kostebek hear word. He feels glad. He runs to light. He meets friend in forest. They hold hand. They say everything be right. They leave place. They walk back home. Good food wait for them.

Kostebek look at world. He sees big world. He walks to place. He finds forest. He stays in forest. He looks around. He sees light. He goes to friend. They walk home. They eat food. They be glad.

Kostebek look at big world again. He finds another place. He walks through old forest. He stays near plant base. He sees light in dark. He feels little inside. He wait for friend. Friend come to him. They walk home together now.

### A2
Poor Kostebek wanted to see the big world outside. He walked far until he reached the Wild Wood. The trees grew big and high around him. The wind made different sounds through the plant parts. Night came fast. The cold dark made him very bad. He stayed under a large tree base. The ground moved. Every little sound caused his heart to hit fast. He was completely alone. He felt a lot of fear in the dark. He did not know what to do. The night was long and hard for him. He wanted to go home now.

Soon, his good friends started looking for him. They called his name. They walked between the old trees with a light. Kostebek heard their voices. He felt a lot of good feeling. He ran fast toward the light. He met his friends in the middle of the area. They held his hands. They told him that everything would be okay. They left the bad place behind. They were happy to find him there. His friends gave him a lot of support. They began to walk back together.

They walked back home. A hot food waited for them. He was happy to see them. They went into the house together. Night was all around them now. He sat down. His friends gave him water and food. He ate everything. He felt very good. He knew he would never go away again. The room was nice. His friends stayed close to him. He looked at their faces. He smiled at them. They talked about the day. The hours passed. He began to feel. His eyes closed. He went to sleep in his bed.

The room was nice. His friends stayed close to him. He looked at their faces. He smiled at them. They talked about the day. The hours passed. He began to feel. His eyes closed. He went to sleep in his bed.

### B1
Poor Kostebek wanted to see the big world outside his small home. He walked far away until he reached a wild place called the Wild Wood. The trees grew thick around him. The wind made strange sounds. He had never visited this part of the country before today. Every step he took brought him deeper into the green forest. He began to wonder if he should turn back toward home. However, he wanted to explore more of the natural environment. He thought that a trip would be a good experience for him. Many different animals lived among the old plants and tall trees. He tried to stay calm while walking through the quiet area. He walked more and more because the trees looked interesting to him. Every plant was green and old. He saw small birds flying above his head. The sun went down slowly behind the high mountains. He wanted to find out what was behind the next group of trees. He kept walking without any fear during the afternoon. The air felt cool and fresh against his face. He loved the quiet nature around him. He did not know that time passed so fast.

Night came quickly. The cold night made him very afraid. He hid under a large wood bed while strange things moved across the ground. Every little noise caused his heart to beat fast because he was alone. He wished that he had never left his warm house. The heavy air made it difficult for him to breathe properly. He closed his eyes and tried to think about happy things. He hoped that morning would arrive soon with bright sun. The silence around him felt heavy and very hard. He pulled his arms close to his body to keep warm. Minutes passed slowly while he waited in the quiet space. He sat on the ground and tried to be very quiet. He heard something moving near his hiding place. His hands shook because he felt so cold and alone. He closed his eyes tightly and waited for the light. He thought about his family and his warm bed at home. He knew that he had made a big mistake by coming here. The dark night seemed like it would never end. Every shadow looked like a monster in the dark. He wished someone would come to help him right now.

Soon, his good friends started looking for him in the dark forest. They called his name strong while walking between the old trees with a light. Kostebek heard their familiar voices and felt very happy. He knew that help had finally arrived for him. His friends had searched everywhere because they cared about his safety. They brought a small light to show the path through the woods. Hearing them made his heart feel light and calm again. He stood up from his hiding spot and cleared his face. He realized that true friends would always support each other. Their presence made him forget all the fear he felt before. They walked through the dark trees and called out his name again. Their voices sounded very close to his hiding spot. He smiled when he heard them calling his name in the dark. He knew they would find him because they were good friends. They carried a bright light to cut through the dark night. The light showed him the way to his friends. He felt a wave of relief wash over his tired body. He stood up slowly from under the wood bed. He was ready to run toward his friends. He knew he was safe now.

He ran quickly toward the light and met his friends in the middle of the woods. They held his hands and told him that everything would be safe now. Together, they left the bad place and walked back home for a warm meal. They talked about the trip while walking along the path. Kostebek promised that he would never walk off alone again. His mother met them at the door with a smile. She served a fine dinner for everyone to enjoy together. They sat around the table and shared stories about the evening. Kostebek felt grateful for his wonderful friends and family. Finally, he went to sleep knowing he was safe at home. The food tasted wonderful after such a long and hard night. His mother gave him a big hug before he went to bed. He climbed into his own warm bed and closed his eyes. He knew he would never go into the Wild Wood alone again. The room was quiet and peaceful. He felt happy to be back home with his family. His friends went back to their own houses after dinner. Kostebek fell asleep fast and slept until morning. He had learned a very important lesson today. Home was always the best place to be.

### B2
Poor Kostebek wanted to see the big world outside his small home. He walked far away until he reached a dark and difficult place called the Wild Wood. The trees grew thick and tall around him, and the wind made strange sounds through the branches. He had never been in such a place before, but his heart was full of hope. Every step he took brought him deeper into the green shadows. The sun went down behind the high hills, and the light faded away completely. Night came quickly, and the cold darkness made him feel very afraid. He hid under a large wood structure while shadows moved across the ground. Every little noise caused his heart to beat fast because he was completely alone. He wished that he could return to his warm house right now. The forest felt like a prison which held him tightly in its hand. He sat on the cold ground and tried to calm his shaking hands. Hours passed slowly while the wind blew through the old trees. He thought about his good friends who were probably worried about him. If they knew where he was, they would come immediately. The silence of the night was broken only by the sounds of strange animals moving in the brush. Kostebek closed his eyes and hoped for morning to arrive quickly. He realized that his adventure was a mistake. The world outside his home was much bigger and more dangerous than he had imagined. He promised himself that he would never run away again if he survived this night. He sat in the dark and waited for many long hours. The cold wind blew across the trees while he thought about his past life. He wondered if his friends would find him before the morning light. Every shadow looked like a monster that wanted to catch him. His hands were shaking because the air was very cold. He tried to keep his mind calm, but fear was everywhere around him. If he had stayed at home, he would be safe now. The forest was a dangerous place where nobody could hear his voice. He pulled his arms close to his body to stay warm. The dark night seemed like it would never end. He looked up at the black sky through the tall branches. No stars could be seen because heavy clouds covered the sky. He whispered his mother name softly in the dark. Tears fell down his face as he remembered his warm bed. The great adventure was nothing like what he had expected. He learned that leaving home without a plan was a terrible choice. He wished he could turn back time and fix his mistake. The silence of the woods made him feel even more small. He promised to be a good boy if he could only see his family again. The hours moved like days while he sat alone on the ground. His heart beat fast every time a branch made a sound. He knew that help might never arrive in such a huge forest. Yet he kept a small spark of hope alive inside his heart.

Soon, his good friends started looking for him in the dark forest. They called his name loudly while walking between the old trees with a light. Kostebek heard their familiar voices and felt a wave of happiness wash over him. He jumped up from the ground and looked toward the bright light. He ran quickly toward the light and met his friends in the middle of the woods. They held his hands and told him that everything would be safe now. Together, they left the difficult place behind and walked back home where a warm meal waited for them. His mother was very glad to see him walk through the front door. She held him tightly and gave him a large bowl of hot food. His friends sat around the table and listened to his story about the Wild Wood. Kostebek learned a very important lesson about staying close to home. He understood that friends are the most valuable thing in life. The dark night was finally over, and a new day began with bright light. He felt tired, but his heart was full of peace. From that day forward, he was happy to stay in his small home and play with the people he loved. The memory of the Wild Wood stayed with him for a long time. He never feared the dark again. He knew his friends would always help him. Life went back to normal, and peace returned to their little corner of the world. His friends smiled and gave him food to help him feel better. His mother kissed his head and thanked the friends for their hard work. They all agreed that staying home was much better than running into danger. Kostebek looked at the people who loved him most. He knew he would never make the same mistake again. The sun rose high in the sky and brought warmth to the little house. Birds sang outside the window as a new morning began. Life was quiet and peaceful once more. He played games with his friends in the garden during the afternoon. The bad memories of the night slowly faded away. He realized how much his family cared about his safety. Every day brought new joy and happiness to his small world. He never wanted to leave his home again. The great adventure was over, and he was finally safe. His heart was full of love for the people around him. He sat by the warm fire and listened to the peaceful sounds outside. The world was big, but his home was the best place of all. He closed his eyes that night with a smile on his face. Nothing could ever break the strong bond between him and his friends.

### C1
Poor Kostebek had long nursed a quiet longing to witness the vast expanse of the world beyond his modest subterranean dwelling. Driven by an irresistible impulse, he walked across the sunlit meadows until he reached the brooding periphery of the Wild Wood. The ancient trees grew thick and imposing around him, their high branches whispering secrets as the wind swept through the canopy. Though he had never ventured into such a forbidding place, his spirit remained buoyed by an innocent and boundless hope. With every deliberate step, he found himself drawn deeper into the shifting emerald shadows of the forest.

The relentless sun sank behind the distant hills, and the ambient daylight faded into a premature and heavy gloom. Night descended with startling swiftness, wrapping the damp landscape in a chilling darkness that instantly awakened his latent fears. Seeking refuge, he huddled beneath a sprawling tangle of roots while ominous shapes seemed to detach themselves from the ground. Every stray rustle and snap of a twig caused his heart to hammer violently against his ribs as the terrifying isolation closed in. He desperately wished that he could instantly transport himself back to the snug safety of his familiar hearth.

The forest felt less like a natural sanctuary and more like a callous prison holding him tightly within its icy grip. He settled onto the frozen earth, desperately attempting to steady his trembling hands and quiet his panicked breathing. Hours dragged by with agonizing slowness while the nocturnal wind moaned through the upper reaches of the ancient timber. His mind drifted to his loyal companions, who were undoubtedly pacing their floors with mounting anxiety on his behalf. He knew with absolute certainty that if they possessed any inkling of his location, they would brave the darkness to rescue him.

The profound silence of the night was shattered only by the furtive movements of unseen creatures rustling through the underbrush. Closing his eyes tightly, Kostebek prayed fervently for the harsh darkness to yield to the first pale streaks of dawn. Only now did he fully appreciate the staggering folly of his impulsive little expedition into the great unknown. The world outside his familiar threshold proved infinitely larger and vastly more perilous than his naive imagination had ever conceived. He swore a silent vow to his own conscience that he would never again abandon the warmth of home should he survive the night.

Hours bled together in an endless procession of cold drafts and phantom terrors that tested the very limits of his endurance. Every looming trunk assumed the menacing silhouette of a watchful monster poised to strike from the shadows. He pulled his woolen coat tightly around his shivering frame, trying vainly to preserve whatever meager body heat remained. Had he exercised better judgment and remained by his hearth, he would be sleeping peacefully right now. The forest remained an indifferent labyrinth where no solitary plea for mercy could ever hope to reach a sympathetic ear.

The black sky above offered no comfort, as an impenetrable ceiling of heavy clouds effectively blotted out every single star. He whispered his mother name into the hollow dark, a frail invocation against the overwhelming weight of the woods. Hot tears traced paths down his cold cheeks as his memory painted vivid pictures of his soft bed and gentle surroundings. He understood now that setting off without preparation was a grave mistake he would regret for the rest of his days. If only he could turn back the relentless clock and undo his reckless departure, he would gladly remain indoors forever.

The oppressive silence magnified his sense of insignificance among the towering giants of the silent timberland. He promised himself to be a model of obedience and gratitude if fortune permitted him to see his family again. Minutes stretched like interminable days while he huddled alone upon the frozen ground, nursing a fragile ember of hope. His racing pulse betrayed his lingering terror whenever a stray branch scraped against another in the rising gale. Though he feared help might never penetrate this vast wilderness, he refused to let the final spark of hope extinguish.

Before long, a faint amber glow pierced the gloom as his loyal friends advanced methodically through the dense undergrowth. They shouted his name aloud, their voices cutting through the nocturnal chill and guiding their steady progress. Hearing those familiar tones wash over him, Kostebek experienced an overwhelming wave of relief and unalloyed joy. He sprang upward from his freezing perch and sprinted toward the welcoming beacon of their approaching lantern. Meeting in the heart of the woods, his companions seized his hands and assured him that the ordeal was finally over.

United once more, they navigated the winding paths out of the formidable wood and returned to the waiting comfort of home. His mother rushed to the doorway, enveloping him in a fierce embrace before serving a steaming bowl of nourishing broth. His friends gathered around the timber table, listening with rapt attention as he recounted his harrowing adventure. Kostebek absorbed the profound lesson of the evening, realizing that true wealth lies in the steadfast devotion of companions. The terrifying night dissolved completely as a gentle morning broke over their peaceful corner of the countryside.

Though physically exhausted, his soul felt entirely at peace as he surveyed the familiar comforts of his sitting room. From that eventful day onward, he felt remarkably content to remain within his cozy domain and cherish his loved ones. The haunting memory of the Wild Wood lingered faintly in his mind, yet it no longer carried the power to frighten him. He understood with absolute clarity that his devoted friends would always stand ready to pull him from any darkness. Order and tranquility were miraculously restored to their little world, banishing every lingering shadow of uncertainty.

His mother kissed his forehead gently and expressed her heartfelt gratitude to the brave companions for their timely rescue. They all concurred heartily that the simple safety of home vastly outweighed the dubious thrills of unknown danger. Sitting beside the crackling hearth in the afternoon, Kostebek played cheerful games and let the bad memories fade away. He felt an immense wave of gratitude for the family and friends who valued his safety above all else. As night fell once more, he closed his eyes with a serene smile, knowing that his adventurous heart had finally found rest.

## st-0239 — Alice: Through the Looking-Glass (epizod)
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Alice sits by the good light. She looks at the large glass. Her body passes through the glass. The room behind is very different. She stands up to look around. Alice feels a little bad. She walks across the room. The room is not right.

She walks down to a place. The small things speak to the girl. People play a game. The white leader talks to Alice. The country is a big game. Alice wants to become a leader. She meets many people. Two men tell a story. A large person appears.

The person looks at Alice. It moves away step by step. Every step brings a new problem. Alice walks through the place. Nobody can remember any names. A white man tries to help. He falls off his animal often. Alice moves toward the line. She wants to return home.

She reaches the end. A fine thing is on her. The leader becomes a little animal. Alice sits up by the light. The time is just a story. She feels very happy now. The morning comes up. Alice sits in the room. She thinks about the event.

### A2
She sat by the fire during the cold night. She looked at the large object above the fire. She wondered what was on the other side. Suddenly, her body passed through the clear wall. The room behind the wall seemed old yet different. She walked down and found a garden with speaking plants. Then she met game pieces moving around a table. A white woman said the country was a huge game. She wanted to reach the final line.

She would become a leader herself then. During the trip, she met many different characters. Two big brothers told a nice story. A large animal appeared in a tree and smiled. It left into the air. Every step forward brought a new problem. She walked through a dark forest. Nobody could remember any names there. A white man tried to help but fell down.

She kept moving forward toward the final line. She wanted to finish the game and return home. She reached the final part. A big and important head cover was placed on her head. The leader turned into a little baby. She opened her eyes by the fire. She realized the whole experience was just a dream. She will always remember the big trip.

The game pieces were very interesting to see. She felt happy about the whole thing. She will play the game again tomorrow. The garden plants were able to speak words. She liked the white woman and the big animal. The dark forest was quite cold during the night. She was happy to return home without risk.

### B1
Alice sat by the warm fire while the cold day slowly turned into evening. She looked at the big glass hanging above the room. She wondered what lay on the other side of the glass. To her great surprise, her body began to pass directly through the glass. The space behind the glass appeared completely different. Yet it was familiar in a strange way. She wanted to know if anything else was hiding behind that window. Every step forward brought a new feeling which she had to consider carefully. The light changed quickly as she moved into the area of the house. She felt that something wonderful might happen at any moment during the day.

When she walked down from the wood, she discovered a yard. All the plants in this yard could speak. The little child also met some strange pieces that were moving around the table. A nice lady told her that the whole country was shaped like a huge game. If Alice could reach the final square, she would become a leader herself. She had never seen anything like this before in her normal life. Each plant had a unique voice and talked about the weather. Alice listened with great interest to every single word they said. The little girl smiled because the plants were so friendly and kind.

During her journey across the strange land, she met many unusual characters along the road. Two fat brothers told her a funny story about a fish and some water. Later, a large animal appeared in a tree. It smiled widely before it slowly disappeared into the air. Every step forward brought a new problem which she had to solve quickly. She wondered how the animal could go so fast without leaving any sign. The brothers kept talking about the fish while walking down the road. Alice did not understand the funny story, but she looked up and kept moving. The road seemed long under the bright sun, but she did not stop.

She walked through a dark forest where nobody could remember any names. A friendly man tried to help her. He kept falling off his horse during the ride. Despite all the trouble, Alice kept moving forward toward the final square of the board. She wanted to finish the game and return home to her warm room. The man on the horse tried to explain the rules, but he fell again. Alice helped him stand up and thanked him for his good effort. The dark forest felt bad, but she did not want to show any fear. She knew that her journey would soon lead her to the end.

At last, she reached the final line. A heavy yellow hat was placed on her head. However, the lady turned into a small baby while she held the piece in her hands. Alice woke up by the fire and realized that the whole story had been a dream. She looked around the room, wondering which world had been real. Her mother walked in and asked if she had slept well by the warm fire. Alice smiled at her mother and remembered the wonderful garden behind the glass. She knew that she would always keep the memory of that strange trip in her heart. It was the best dream she had ever experienced in her whole life.

### B2
Alice sat by the warm place where a bright fire burned while the winter afternoon slowly turned into evening. She looked at the large mirror hanging above the heat and wondered what lay on the other side of the glass. To her great surprise, her body began to pass directly through the glass surface. The room behind the mirror appeared completely different, yet it was familiar in a strange way. She wanted to know if the world inside the glass was real. Every single moment brought a new adventure which she had to face with strong will. The young girl felt that something extraordinary was going to happen in this place. She always liked to discover new things when she went outside into unknown areas. If she stayed longer, she might find more answers about this wonderful land. The winter wind blew outside, but inside the room felt warm and safe.

When she walked down from the high shelf, she discovered a garden where all the flowers could speak. The little girl also met some strange board pieces that were moving around the table. A white lady leader told her that the whole country was shaped like a huge game of board play. If Alice could reach the final square, she would become a leader herself. The plants talked about many different subjects while the sun shone brightly above them. Alice listened carefully because she wanted to understand every single word they said. The garden was filled with bright flowers that grew tall and strong. Some flowers asked her questions about where she came from and who her family was. She answered them very well before she continued her long journey across the land. The path ahead seemed long, but she felt ready for whatever came next.

During her journey across the strange land, she met many unusual characters along the road. Two fat brothers told her a funny story about a sea animal and some little fish. Later, a large cat appeared in a tree, smiling widely before it slowly disappeared into the air. Every step forward brought a new problem which she had to solve quickly. The two brothers were very funny and made her laugh with their silly words. The smiling cat disappeared completely until only its wide grin remained visible in the sky. Alice wondered how such a strange thing could ever happen in real life. She kept walking along the brown road while the afternoon sun began to drop lower. The journey was long and tiring, but she refused to stop or turn back. Every new encounter made her realize how different this world was from her home.

She walked through a dark forest where nobody could remember any names. A white soldier tried to help her, but he kept falling off his horse during the ride. Despite all the confusion, Alice kept moving forward toward the final square of the board. She wanted to finish the game and return home to her warm room. The trees in the forest were tall and blocked most of the light from the sky. The soldier was very kind, even though he was not skilled and kept losing his balance. Alice helped him up whenever he fell down from his animal. They talked about the game and how important it was to reach the end safely. The forest was quiet, and the air smelled like old wood and green leaves. She felt glad to have someone to talk to while she walked through the shadows.

At last, she reached the final line and a heavy golden circle was placed on her head. However, the lady leader turned into a little baby animal while she held the piece in her hands. Alice woke up by the warm place and realized that the whole adventure had been a dream. She looked around the room, wondering which world had been real. The gold object felt heavy and bright as she balanced it carefully upon her hair. Then everything changed in a flash, and she was holding a soft little animal instead. Her eyes opened wide as she found herself sitting back in her own comfortable chair. The fire was still burning softly, and the room looked exactly as it had before. She smiled to herself while she thought about all the amazing things she had seen. It was a wonderful dream, but she was also happy to be back home again.

### C1
Alice curled herself comfortably by the hearth, where a bright fire crackled while the winter afternoon slowly dissolved into evening. She gazed upward at the large, ornate mirror hanging above the mantelpiece, wondering what peculiar realm might lie hidden on the other side of the glass. To her utter astonishment, her body began to pass directly through the solid silvered surface as though it were nothing more than a curtain of mist. The room reflected behind the glass appeared completely inverted and unfamiliar, yet it retained a strange, unsettling echo of her own familiar parlor. Driven by an insatiable curiosity, she resolved to discover whether this curious world possessed any tangible reality.

Every single moment brought a fresh, unexpected adventure which she had to face with a resolute and unwavering will. The young girl instinctively felt that something extraordinary was about to unfold within this silent, frozen domain. She had always possessed a restless spirit, eagerly seeking to explore uncharted territories whenever the opportunity arose. Had she chosen to remain by her fireside, she would never have uncovered the myriad secrets hidden within this enchanted land. Outside, the winter wind howled against the windowpanes, but inside the looking-glass room, the air felt strangely still and expectant.

Stepping down carefully from the high mantel shelf, she soon discovered a wondrous garden where all the vibrant flowers possessed the gift of speech. Not far away, she also encountered several peculiar chess pieces that were moving purposefully across a large table. A stately White Queen informed her that the entire country was laid out in the precise pattern of a gigantic game of chess. If only Alice could summon the courage to reach the final square, she would be granted the high honor of becoming a queen herself. The surrounding plants babbled animatedly about a multitude of subjects while the sun shone brightly upon their colorful petals.

Alice listened with rapt attention, for she wanted to absorb every single word the articulate blossoms uttered. The garden was densely populated with tall, strong flowers that swayed gracefully in the gentle breeze. Several curious roses paused to question her about her origins, asking where she had come from and to what family she belonged. Having answered their polite inquiries with characteristic grace, she continued her long and arduous journey across the checkerboard landscape. Although the path ahead looked daunting, she felt fully prepared for whatever trials awaited her next.

As she traveled further into the strange territory, she encountered numerous eccentric characters roaming along the winding road. Two plump brothers crossed her path, cheerfully reciting a nonsensical tale concerning a curious sea creature and a school of tiny fish. Shortly after they vanished, a large Cheshire cat materialized in the boughs of an overhead tree, flashing a wide, mischievous grin before slowly fading into the open air. Every step forward introduced a fresh enigma that she was forced to navigate with clever resourcefulness. The two brothers had been delightfully amusing, leaving her chuckling at their absurd rhymes.

The grinning cat dissolved gradually until nothing remained of its presence save for a lingering, disembodied smile suspended against the sky. Alice pondered how such an impossible phenomenon could ever manifest outside the boundaries of a dream. She continued walking steadily along the dusty brown road while the afternoon sun began its slow descent toward the horizon. Though the journey was physically exhausting, she steadfastly refused to abandon her quest or turn back toward safety. Each bizarre encounter served to remind her just how profoundly this surreal domain diverged from her ordinary home.

Before long, her travels brought her into the shadows of a dense, shadowy forest where memory itself seemed to dissolve into nothingness. Within this quiet woodland, no living creature could recall its own proper name. A clumsy White Knight soon appeared, attempting to offer assistance, though he repeatedly tumbled headfirst from his mount at the slightest movement. Despite the profound confusion that surrounded them, Alice pressed onward, keeping her eyes fixed on the distant final square of the board. She desperately desired to complete the game and return safely to her cozy, warm room.

The ancient trees towered overhead, blocking out the majority of the golden sunlight. The well-meaning knight proved remarkably inept at remaining upright, yet his gentle disposition made his frequent falls endearing rather than frustrating. Whenever he tumbled into the ferns, Alice kindly helped him back into his saddle, dusting off his heavy armor. They conversed amiably about the peculiar nature of the giant game and the critical importance of reaching the end unharmed. The forest was remarkably hushed, smelling richly of damp earth, old timber, and crushed green leaves.

Finding herself in such tranquil company made the shadowy march feel far less lonely than it might have otherwise. At last, after what felt like an eternity of walking, she crossed the threshold of the final line. Instantly, a heavy golden crown materialized and was placed gently upon her brow. To her utter bewilderment, the dignified White Queen suddenly transformed into a soft, wriggling infant animal while resting in her hands. Alice clutched the precious metal object, marveling at the strange alchemy of the moment.

The golden circlet felt surprisingly heavy and bright as she balanced it carefully upon her hair. Then, in a blinding flash of light, the heavy metal dissolved into the warm fur of a living creature. Her eyes flew wide open as she realized she was no longer standing in a forest, but sitting comfortably in her own armchair. The fireplace was still radiating a soft, steady warmth, and the parlor appeared entirely unchanged. A quiet smile touched her lips as she reflected upon the remarkable journey she had just completed.

It had undoubtedly been the most extraordinary dream imaginable, yet a part of her was deeply relieved to be back in the familiar world. She stroked the soft fabric of her dress, half expecting a chess piece to leap out from behind the sofa. The winter wind still rattled the glass panes outside, but the room felt secure and peaceful. She closed her eyes for a brief moment, letting the fading images of the looking-glass realm drift away into sleep. Home had never felt quite so sweet.
