## st-0248 — The Tortoise and the Eagle
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One day, a little one wants to go up. He looks at other things in the open world. His life on the land is not fast. He wants to see more every day. He looks all around him with care. The little one watches things go up high. He has no way to go up. He wants to experience something new and big. His mind has a strong hope. He thinks about the open wind.

A big strong one comes down to him. The little one asks the big one for help. The big one says little ones cannot go up. The little one asks again and again. He does not stop asking for help. The big one takes him up high. They go over small houses and open rooms. They move through the open wind. The little one feels very glad. He looks down at the world. He does not think about his body.

The big one lets go of him now. The little one goes down very fast. He lands on a big hard floor. The little one feels bad from the fall. He does not want to go up again. He stays on the land from then on. He walks down every way with care. He knows his place is on the land. He never tries to go up again. He lives his life right there.

### A2
Once, a little animal had a great wish to fly. He watched other animals high up in the blue space every day. He wanted to join them. Life on the ground felt too plain and boring. He wanted to see the world from above like strong animals.

One afternoon, a strong animal flew down near the little one. The animal went to the other animal and asked for help. He explained his deep wish to fly across the air. The animal looked at him with surprise. The animal tried to explain that animals cannot fly. However, the little animal would not listen to any word.

He asked the animal again and again. Finally, the animal agreed to try. The animal picked up the little animal with strong feet. They began to rise into the air. They flew high above trees, green land, and town houses.

The animal felt a great sense of happy feeling. Wind moved around him. He looked down at the land. He thought he was the most successful animal. He forgot he had no arms. His huge house could not support him. He started to make sound with happy feeling.

Suddenly, the animal let go of him. The strange person was moving too much. The animal began to fall fast through open air. He headed down toward hard land. He realized his big problem too late. With a strong hit, he landed on a large rock. He learned a very hard thing.

From that day on, the animal never wanted to fly. He stayed on the ground where he stayed. He walked through green land. The experience taught him a thing. Every animal has its own place. Wishing for things beyond natural ability brings trouble.

### B1
Once upon a time, a small animal had a big wish to fly up. He spent every day watching birds high above the green hills and town houses. The small animal felt that life on the ground was too slow for him. He had always wanted to see the world from high above. One afternoon, a strong bird landed near the small animal on the ground. The small animal asked the big bird for help with his secret wish. The big bird explained that animals of his kind were not made for flying. However, the small animal would not listen to any good advice from his friend. The big bird agreed to try and took the small animal up. They flew high above the tall trees and the small town houses. The small animal felt glad as the wind blew around him in the air. He forgot that his heavy body could not support him up in the open sky.

He started to shout with extreme happiness, believing he was a bird like the others. Suddenly, the big bird let go because the small animal moved too much. The small animal began to fall fast toward the hard ground below. He realized his mistake too late before a heavy crash against a large stone. From that day on, the small animal never wanted to fly again in his life. He has stayed on the ground and walked slowly through the green plants. The experience has taught him that every living thing has its proper place in the world. Many days have passed since that terrible accident happened in the open field near the town. If the small animal tries to fly again, he will surely fall down. Therefore, he always stays close to the safe earth and walks slowly every day. He has learned that life on the ground is the best place for him to live.

Other animals in the area have seen what happened to him during that strange flight. They also know that flying is only for birds in the sky. If anyone asks the small animal about his trip, he just smiles and shakes his head. He has never forgotten the lessons of that afternoon when he tried to reach high. The world is full of wonderful things to see. He does not need to leave the safety of the green trees. Every animal must accept his own natural limits and be glad with what he has. He will never go up into the open sky again. The ground is his home and he will stay there for the rest of his life. Life is good when you walk on the green earth and look at the trees. He knows that the ground is the right place for an animal like him. That is his story and he will always remember it well.

### B2
Once upon a time, there lived a little creature who had a great desire to fly. He spent every day watching birds high up in the blue sky, wishing that he could join them. The animal felt that his life on the ground was too slow and boring. He wanted to see the world from above, just like the large birds did. One afternoon, a strong bird flew down and landed near the creature. The animal went to the big creature and asked for help. He explained his deep wish to fly across the clouds and see distant places. The bird looked at him with surprise and tried to explain that animals were not made for flying. However, the creature would not listen to any reason or good advice. He asked the bird again and again until the creature finally agreed to try. If the animal had known the danger, he would have stayed down. If the bird had not helped him, he would not have fallen. If the creature were smarter, he would always walk on the ground. The bird, who was very wise, watched the animal from a tall tree.

The bird picked up the animal with his strong feet and began to rise into the air. They flew high above the trees, the green hills, and the small houses of the town. The creature felt an amazing sense of happiness as the wind blew around him. He looked down at the earth and thought that he was the most successful animal alive. He forgot that he had no wings of his own and that his heavy shell could not support him in the sky. He started to shout with joy, believing that he was now a bird. Suddenly, the bird let go of him because the silly creature was moving too much. The animal began to fall fast through the open air, heading straight down toward the hard ground. He realized his big mistake too late, wishing that he had stayed safe on the earth. With a loud crash, the poor creature landed on a large stone, learning a very hard lesson about desire and reality. People in the nearby town often talk about the day the animal tried to fly. This story remains an important lesson for anyone who wishes for impossible things.

From that day on, the animal never wanted to fly again. He stayed on the ground where he belonged and walked slowly through the grass. The experience taught him that every creature has its own proper place in the world. He understood that wishing for things beyond one natural ability only brings trouble. The animal walked through the green grass every morning. He thought about the flight that had caused him so much pain. The big bird watched him from high above in the branches. The creature knew that he must never leave the ground again. His heavy shell was meant for walking, not for flying through the clouds. Other animals looked at him and understood the lesson. Life on the earth was good enough for everyone who lived there. The story of the foolish flight was told by the villagers for many years.

### C1
Once upon a time, within the quiet valleys of a timeless land, there lived a humble tortoise who possessed an immense and restless desire to fly. He spent every endless day watching majestic birds soar high up in the brilliant blue sky, wishing fiercely that he could join them. The terrestrial animal felt that his life upon the solid ground was far too slow and remarkably boring. He hungered to see the world from high above, just like the large birds did every single afternoon. That heavy shell upon his back felt less like a protective home and more like a heavy burden anchoring him to the dust.

One particularly warm afternoon, a powerful eagle flew down from the clouds and landed near the curious creature. Seizing the rare moment, the tortoise walked directly to the magnificent bird and politely asked for assistance. He explained his deep, burning wish to fly across the open clouds and witness distant places that no ground-dwelling soul had ever seen. The eagle looked down at him with genuine surprise, tilting his sharp head to study the strange petitioner. With a cautious voice, the raptor tried to explain that tortoises were simply not made for the freedom of the air.

However, the stubborn creature would not listen to any reason or accept any good advice from his majestic visitor. He asked the bird again and again with relentless persistence until the eagle finally agreed to try the impossible experiment. If the tortoise had known the terrible danger awaiting him in the winds, he would have stayed firmly upon the grass. If the eagle had not relented and helped him, the tragic fall would never have happened at all. If the creature possessed even a shred of true wisdom, he would have remained satisfied with walking the earth.

Perched high on the sturdy branch of a tall oak tree, the wise eagle watched the tortoise prepare for his great journey. The great bird reached down, grasped the heavy animal firmly in his strong talons, and began to rise into the air. They swept high above the green trees, the rolling green hills, and the small, distant houses of the nearby town. The tortoise felt an astonishing, electric sense of pure happiness as the cool wind rushed loudly around his face. He looked down at the shrinking earth below and thought that he must be the most successful animal alive.

In that intoxicating moment of dizzying triumph, he completely forgot that he had no wings of his own. His heavy, solid shell could not possibly support him in the thin air without the assistance of his carrier. Overcome by sudden vanity, he started to shout with loud joy, firmly believing that he had somehow transformed into a bird. He demanded that the eagle acknowledge his newfound status as master of the sky, gesturing wildly with his stubby legs. Only a fool would dance upon the edge of an abyss, yet the tortoise rejoiced while dangling miles above the stones.

Suddenly, irritated by the chaotic movement and the foolish noise, the eagle abruptly let go of his clumsy passenger. The silly creature began to fall fast through the open air, heading straight down toward the hard, unforgiving ground. Panic replaced pride in an instant as the rush of wind turned from a sweet song into a terrifying roar. He realized his enormous mistake far too late, wishing desperately that he had stayed safe and sound upon the earth. Gravity reclaimed its own with ruthless efficiency, offering no mercy to the dreamer who defied the natural order.

With a loud, resounding crash that echoed across the valley, the poor creature landed squarely on a large, jagged stone. The impact shattered his illusions entirely, teaching him a very hard, painful lesson about desire and reality. People in the nearby town often talk about that strange afternoon when the land-bound animal tried to touch the clouds. This dramatic story remains an important, sobering lesson for anyone who foolishly wishes for things that cannot be. Bruised and aching, the survivor lay motionless for a long time while the quiet wind whispered through the grass.

From that exact day onward, the chastened animal never wanted to fly again, not even for a fleeting second. He stayed permanently on the ground where he belonged, walking slowly and carefully through the green grass. The harrowing experience taught him a profound truth: every living creature has its own proper place in the world. He finally understood that wishing for things far beyond one natural ability only brings unnecessary trouble and grief. No longer would he gaze upward with bitterness; instead, he accepted the quiet peace of the solid earth beneath his feet.

Every single morning, the tortoise walked slowly through the familiar meadows, feeling the reassuring texture of the soil. He often thought about the disastrous flight that had caused him so much physical pain and emotional regret. High above in the branches of the old trees, the great eagle would occasionally watch him from a distance. The tortoise knew with absolute certainty that he must never leave the safety of the ground again. His heavy, rounded shell was clearly meant for slow walking, not for soaring carelessly through the distant clouds.

Other local animals looked at him often and quietly understood the deep moral lesson hidden within his misfortune. Life on the earth was more than good enough for everyone who lived there without chasing impossible dreams. The story of the foolish flight was told and retold by the local villagers for many long years. Parents shared the tale with their children to ensure they learned the value of patience and self-acceptance. Wisdom, after all, is frequently bought through bitter experience and the sudden shattering of pride.

As the sun began to set behind the hills, casting long shadows across the peaceful valley, the tortoise found his rest. He pulled his head safely inside his strong shell, feeling secure in the quiet twilight of the darkening world. The sky above turned from brilliant orange to deep indigo, filled with genuine birds returning to their nests. He felt no envy toward them now, only a quiet respect for the natural boundaries that kept them all safe. Some dreams are better left unfulfilled, and some journeys are best measured by the wisdom of return.

## st-0249 — The Boy and the Filberts
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A young child walks near a house. The child walks every day. He walks by a room. He sees an open table. An open room has a place. The child looks around the room. He sees an open thing. The open thing has food. The food is good. The child wants the good food. He wants to eat the food. He walks to the open thing. He puts his hand down. He puts his hand into the thing. The thing holds good food. The child takes a lot of food. He holds many food parts in hand.

His hand is too big now. The opening is very small. He wants to move his hand. He tries to take his hand out. He cannot move his hand out. The child stands by the table. He feels very bad. He begins to feel bad. His hand remains still. His hand stays in the thing. The child makes a sound. He has a problem now.

A friend walks into the room. The friend looks at the child. The friend sees the problem. The friend gives good help. The friend tells him something. The child hears the friend. The child thinks about the help. The child opens his hand. He lets go of some food. He leaves half of the food. His hand comes out of the thing. His hand is free now. The child feels very well.

### A2
Once a young boy walked near a large house. The boy looked inside a room. A nice table stood in the room. A large item stood on the table. The item was full of food. The food was sweet filberts. The food was good and fine. The boy saw the open item. He wanted food very much. He walked inside without fear. He put his hand deep into the item. His hand took many pieces of food. He took as many pieces as he could hold. He tried to pull his hand back out. His closed hand was too big for the opening.

He could not pull his hand out of the item. If he had taken fewer pieces, his hand would come out. Instead, he held every single piece. The poor boy stood by the table. He felt very bad. He did not know what to do. He began to make a sound. His hand was caught in the jar. His problem caused a bad result. A friend from the house walked into the room. The kind friend saw the boy by the table. The friend gave him a simple piece of direction. The person told him to let go of half the food.

The boy listened and dropped half of the food. His hand became smaller. Then his hand came out. He was free. He still had enough food. He learned that taking less is better than losing everything. He was going to remember this game for a long time. He walked away from the house with a smile. The day was good after all.

### B1
Once upon a time, a young boy walked near a large garden where a house stood. Inside that house, a nice glass container was placed upon a brown table. The container was completely filled with sweet nuts. The young boy looked inside the room and saw the open container. Because he wanted something good to eat, he walked inside without any fear. He put his hand deep into the narrow neck of the glass container. His fingers held as many food pieces as he could hold in his hand. He tried to pull his hand back out with all the food. However, his closed hand was now too big for the narrow opening. He could not pull his hand out because the hole was too small. If he had taken only a few pieces, his hand would have slipped back out. Instead, he held onto every single piece that he had held. The poor boy stood there by the table, feeling very sad and confused. He began to cry a little bit because his hand was stuck fast. His desire for too much had caused a funny problem which he could not solve alone.

After some time, a friend who lived in the house walked into the room. The kind friend saw the boy standing by the table with his hand caught. The good friend looked at the situation and gave him a simple piece of advice. The person told the child to let go of half the food immediately. The boy listened to this good suggestion and dropped half of the items. As soon as his hand became smaller, his arm easily came out of the container. He was finally free, and he still had enough food left to enjoy. The child learned that taking less at one time is better than losing everything. He has never forgotten this important lesson about life. If he ever sees another open container, he will remember his past mistake. His friend has also told other people about the funny event. Everyone in the house now laughs when they talk about the day. The young boy always shares his food with others now. He has shared many meals since that day. If he sees someone else in trouble, he will help them right away.

### B2
Once upon a time, a young boy walked near a large garden where a house stood. Inside that house, a nice glass container was placed upon a wooden table. The container was completely filled with a huge amount of wonderful sweet seeds. The boy looked inside the room and saw the open container. Because he wanted something good to eat, he walked inside without any fear. He put his hand deep into the narrow neck of the glass container. His fingers grabbed as many seeds as he could possibly hold in his hand. He tried to pull his hand back out with all the food.

However, his closed hand filled with too many seeds was now too big for the narrow opening. He could not pull his hand out because the glass hole was too small for such a big cluster. If he had taken only a few seeds, his hand would have easily slipped back out. Instead, he held onto every single piece that he had grabbed. The poor boy stood there by the table, feeling very sad and confused. He began to cry a little bit because his hand was stuck fast. He could neither move his arm forward nor pull it away from the container. His strong desire for food had caused a silly problem which he could not solve alone.

After some time, another person who lived in the house walked into the room. The kind friend saw the boy standing by the table with his hand trapped. The wise friend looked at the situation and gave him a very simple piece of advice. The person told the child to let go of half the seeds immediately. The boy listened to this good suggestion and dropped half of the seeds from his hand. As soon as his hand became smaller, his hand easily came out of the container. He was finally free, and he still had enough wonderful food left to enjoy. The child learned that taking less at one time is often much better than losing everything.

Every single day, children grow up and learn important lessons about life and desire. If a person wants too much at once, trouble will surely follow. Wise people always understand that sharing and careful control bring peace. The boy never forgot that day by the wooden table in the quiet house. Whenever he faced a difficult choice, he remembered the stuck hand. He would smile and think about how little things matter when freedom is safe. Life offers many paths, but the right path is always chosen with care. Thus, the old story remains a wonderful example for anyone who reads it today.

Long ago, stories were told by wise older people to teach the young ones about proper behavior. This particular story about the glass container and the seeds has traveled through many generations. People everywhere enjoy listening to tales that reveal human nature so clearly. When children hear such a narrative, they realize that taking too much creates serious trouble. If everyone practiced careful control, our world would be a much better place for everyone. We must always remember to keep our desires under control during difficult moments. The wise friend in the house showed how simple advice can solve complex problems quickly. Therefore, we should always listen carefully when good people offer helpful words.

Sometimes, a person might become so focused on getting something that nothing else matters. That kind of intense drive can blind us to obvious solutions. If the boy had only let go of the extra seeds sooner, he would have avoided all that pain. Instead, he stood there crying until someone else came to help him out. We often need friends to show us the right way when we feel stuck. Support from others is a great gift that makes our journey through life much easier. Let us always be grateful for the people who help us during hard times. May we also be ready to assist anyone who faces a similar problem in the future.

Long ago, a wise person wrote many books about human life and nature. These books were shared among families to help children grow into good adults. Every generation needs to hear such tales to understand the world better. When people read these old stories, they learn valuable lessons about behavior and choice. If someone makes a mistake, others should help them find a correct path. Friends are always there to support us when we face difficult situations. We must appreciate every person who brings light into our daily lives. Let us remember that simple actions often lead to great success.

In our modern world, people face many challenges every single day. Sometimes we want too much money or too many things at the same time. If we chase too many goals at once, we might lose everything we have built. Wise leaders always advise their teams to keep a balance in all matters. If anyone follows this advice, they will find peace and happiness easily. Life is short, so we should spend our time on what truly matters. Let us keep our minds open and our hearts full of warmth for everyone. Then our future will remain bright and full of wonderful opportunities.

### C1
Twilight had begun to stretch long shadows across the quiet valley when the young boy wandered near the grand estate. Behind the wrought iron fence stood a magnificent garden, tended with meticulous care and bordered by an ancient stone wall. Within the stately house that rose beyond the greenery, a polished wooden table sat bathed in the fading amber light of late afternoon. Resting upon its heavy surface was a clear glass vessel of remarkable beauty, catching the dying sunbeams in its curves. To the wandering child, peering intently through the open window, the object of greatest fascination was the abundance contained within. The heavy glass jar was filled to the brim with sweet roasted filberts, their rich brown shells gleaming faintly through the smooth walls. Having spent the afternoon running through the dusty lanes, the boy felt a sharp pang of hunger grip his stomach. Without a second thought for the boundaries of privacy or property, he slipped through the open casement window into the silent room. His bare feet made no sound against the polished floorboards as he approached the heavy table like a shadow. His eyes remained fixed upon the delicious prize, completely blind to anything beyond the immediate gratification of his appetite. With breathless anticipation, he thrust his hand deep down into the narrow neck of the glass container. His eager fingers stretched wide, scraping against the hard sides of the jar as they plunged toward the bottom. There lay the sweet harvest, piled richly in a dense mound that promised an absolute feast. Without hesitation, he curled his fingers inward, scooping up as many filberts as his palm could possibly press together. He grabbed a massive handful, confident that his prize was secure within his grip. Triumph filled his chest as he prepared to pull the heavy harvest back out into the open air. Yet, the moment he attempted to withdraw his arm, a sudden and stubborn obstacle brought his movement to a dead halt. His tightly clenched fist, bulging with the heavy cluster of nuts, was now significantly wider than the narrow opening of the jar. No matter how hard he pulled, his knuckles jammed firmly against the rigid glass rim, refusing to pass through. The neck of the container had not changed its dimensions, but his own greed had transformed his hand into an inescapable trap. Panic fluttered in his chest as he tugged harder, pulling with all his might against the unyielding barrier.

The afternoon silence of the room was broken only by the sound of his frantic breathing and the dull scrape of skin against glass. He pulled left, then right, twisting his wrist at awkward angles, but the narrow neck held him fast in its transparent grip. Had he been willing to sacrifice even a fraction of his haul, his hand would have slipped out effortlessly into the freedom of the room. Instead, driven by an unyielding impulse of acquisition, he held on desperately to every single nut he had initially seized. Not a single filbert would he willingly surrender to the floor, even as his predicament grew increasingly dire. The initial thrill of the harvest dissolved rapidly into a cold realization of helplessness and sharp physical discomfort. Tears welled in his eyes, hot and sudden, tracing clean paths down his dust-stained cheeks as he stood trapped beside the heavy wooden table. He could neither push his arm back down to release his grip nor pull it upward to escape the glass prison. His own stubborn desire had forged invisible chains that bound him securely to the spot. Every second that ticked by felt like an eternity, magnifying his foolishness in the empty, echoing room. Footsteps sounded softly from the hallway outside, breaking the heavy stillness that had settled over the distressed child. A resident of the house, a person of gentle demeanor and quiet wisdom, stepped into the sunlit room to investigate the faint noises. Pausing by the doorway, the visitor took in the strange scene with a calm and understanding gaze. The boy stood frozen beside the table, his arm raised at an awkward angle, weeping quietly into the empty space. It required no great detective work to comprehend the nature of the crisis that had befallen the young intruder. Approaching with slow and reassuring steps, the kind friend offered neither scolding nor harsh judgment for the intrusion. Instead, pointing gently toward the trapped hand within the narrow glass neck, the wise observer offered a remarkably simple solution. The advice was as brief as it was profound: let go of half the nuts immediately, and the problem would vanish. The boy stared at the speaker, his mind wrestling briefly with the painful prospect of losing half his hard-earned prize. Yet, feeling the growing ache in his strained muscles, he realized he had no other viable choice remaining.

Slowly, his fingers relaxed their fierce tension, allowing several of the round brown filberts to tumble free and scatter across the table. As the volume of his captured treasure shrank, the shape of his fist altered instantly within the narrow glass neck. The bulky mass dissolved into a manageable size, and with a sudden, smooth motion, his hand slipped effortlessly out of the container. He staggered backward a step, rubbing his sore wrist, staring in astonishment at the small handful of nuts that remained in his palm. He was completely free at last, and the remaining food was more than enough to satisfy his hunger. That singular afternoon left an indelible mark upon the growing boy, shaping his understanding of the world long after the tears had dried. As the years progressed and he encountered new trials, the memory of his stuck hand returned to him with comforting clarity. Whenever ambition threatened to overwhelm his judgment, he would recall the narrow neck of the jar and the folly of holding on too tightly. Life presented countless corridors of choice, and he learned that true wisdom lay in knowing what to release. The simple domestic incident became a permanent compass for his moral and personal decisions. The wisdom born from such everyday mistakes has traveled across generations, preserved in the oral traditions of thoughtful communities everywhere. When children hear this timeless narrative, they recognize the dangerous nature of unbridled appetite long before they face the harsher trials of adulthood. A society that values moderation avoids the needless suffering brought about by excess and blind ambition. The kind friend in the house demonstrated that complex human struggles often yield to the simplest truths when properly applied. Therefore, attentive listeners always cherish the quiet guidance offered by those who have walked the path before them. Ultimately, the story of the boy and the filberts remains a quiet monument to human nature and the art of living well. It reminds every passerby that freedom and peace are infinitely more valuable than the accumulation of unnecessary burdens. As long as people gather to share stories in the fading evening light, this cautionary tale will continue to offer its gentle instruction. Let us therefore keep our desires measured and our minds open to the simple truths that surround us daily. In doing so, we navigate the complexities of our existence with grace, ensuring that our hands remain free for whatever truly matters.

## st-0250 — The Two Pots
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago, two things live near water. One thing is made of old earth. The other thing is made of hard iron. The iron part is big. The earth part is small. One day, the iron part looks down. He looks at the earth part. He asks to go into the water. The earth part feels bad. Water can break it.

The iron part makes a good promise. He will stay close. He will help his friend. Both things go into the water. They move with the water. The water is good. They move together. The water is clear. Then the water becomes fast.

The iron part tries to stay close. The water moves them away. The earth part meets a hard thing. The earth part is not strong. The little part breaks into many small bits. The iron part cannot help his friend. The earth part goes down. It is gone.

People must choose company with care. Do not take bad events. Know your own bounds well. We always live with care every day. We must see the water. We must know the way. Good friends stay close to us.

### A2
Long ago, two things lived near a large water. One thing was made of mud. The other thing was strong and made of business material. The material thing was heavy. The mud thing was light and easy to break. One day, the material thing looked at the mud thing. He suggested a long travel down the water. The mud thing felt bad. Water could easily break it. However, the material thing promised to protect his friend. He promised to stay close to him. Both things entered the water. They began to go down the water together. The current was nice. They moved together across the clear surface. After some time, the water became fast and hard. This happened near a small and difficult area. The material thing tried to move closer. The heavy water pushed them apart. The mud thing was pulled hard against a hard piece of ground. The mud thing was not strong. The hit broke the little thing into many pieces quickly. The material thing could not save his friend. He tried to reach the broken pieces. It was already too late. The mud thing went down deep into the dark water. It was gone now.

This story shows an important thing. People who are not strong must always choose company well. If you are not strong, you should stay away from bad things. Do this with strong friends. A person who knows his own limits is good. He avoids bad risks. Real protection comes from understanding your abilities. The material thing is going to remember this day. The mud thing is going to stay under the water. People are going to tell this story for a long time. Friends must always help each other. Do not go into fast water if you are not strong. Always think before you make a big decision. Life is short and full of bad things. We must be very good every day. Good friends will always protect you from damage. Bad choices bring bad results. That is the end of this old story. We should always keep our friends safe. Life can bring many difficult problems. People must learn from old stories. Friends are very important in this world. Do not take big risks with bad company. Always think about your friends before you move. The water can be very dangerous for small things. Be sure to stay with good people every day.

### B1
Long ago, two things lived near a large river. One item was made from earth. The other was made from strong metal. The metal item was heavy. The earth item was light. One day, the metal item looked at the earth item. He suggested a long trip down the river. The earth item felt afraid because water could easily break it.

However, the metal item promised that he would stay close. He promised to protect his friend. With that promise, both items entered the water. They began to move. The current was quiet. They moved together across the bright surface. After some time, the river became fast near a narrow area. The metal item tried to swim closer. The heavy water pushed them apart.

The earth item was pulled hard against a sharp stone in the river. Because the earth was weak, the event broke the little item into many pieces immediately. The metal item could not save his friend from the danger. Although the metal item tried to reach the broken pieces, it was already too late. The earth item went deep down into the dark water and disappeared.

This story shows that weak people must always choose their company carefully. If you are not strong, you should stay away from dangerous situations with powerful friends. A person knows his own limits and avoids bad risks. Real safety comes from understanding what you can and cannot do.

Many years have passed since that day by the water. People still tell this story to their children in every town. If someone asks you about the two friends, you will remember the lesson. Every child has heard how the metal item failed to save the earth item. We must always think about our own strength before we make a choice.

Life brings many challenges that we must face each day. If we make bad choices, we might experience great loss. Therefore, we should find good friends who are similar to us. When people stay with the right group, they find safety and peace. This simple truth has guided human society through many ages.

Let us consider how we live our lives every single day. Do we run into danger without thinking about the result? Or do we take care to protect ourselves from damage? A careful person always plans ahead to avoid trouble. If you want to live a long life, you must be well prepared for your choices.

The river continues to flow past the green trees and the old stones. No one has ever seen the earth item return from the deep water. The metal item learned a hard lesson about company and responsibility. We also can learn from their sad experience today. May we always remember to stay safe and choose our friends well.

### B2
Long ago, two pots lived near a large river. One pot was made from hard earth material, while the other was made from strong metal. The metal pot was heavy, but the other pot was light and easily broken. One bright day, the metal pot looked at the other pot and suggested a long journey down the stream. The pot felt nervous because water could easily break it. However, the metal pot promised that he would stay close and protect his friend. With that promise, both pots entered the water and began to float along. The current was gentle, and they moved together across the bright surface. After some time, the river became fast and rough near a narrow area of stones. The metal pot tried to swim closer, but the heavy water pushed them apart. The pot was pulled hard against a sharp stone in the river. Because the material was weak, the sudden force broke the little pot into many pieces immediately. The metal pot could not save his friend from the danger. Although the metal pot tried to reach the broken pieces, it was already too late. The pot sank deep down into the dark water and disappeared forever.

This story shows that weak people must always choose their company carefully. If you are not strong, you should stay away from dangerous situations with powerful friends. A smart person knows his own limits and avoids bad risks. Real safety comes from understanding what you can and cannot do. People have discussed this journey for a long time. Many children hear this tale when they are young. They learn that strong friends can sometimes bring trouble if life becomes difficult. We must always look at our own strength before we make big plans. If someone is not careful, he might find himself in serious trouble. Therefore, a smart person stays with those who share the same level of strength. Life is full of risks which must be managed with care. When we make choices, we should think about the future. That is why this ancient story remains important today. Everyone can learn a valuable lesson from the two pots.

Many years ago, people started to tell this story to every child. The tale spread across the country and became famous among many families. If the metal pot had not suggested the journey, the little pot would have stayed safe. However, the little pot trusted the promise which was given by the metal friend. That trust led to a terrible result when the rough water arrived. We can see how important it is to know our own strength. If someone is weak, that person should not try to act like someone who is strong. Friends who are very powerful might create problems for those who are less strong. This lesson is taught in many schools so that young people learn about life. When decisions are made, careful thought must be applied to every single risk. If you do not think about the future, danger will find you. That is why this ancient story is still shared today. We must remember that real protection comes from knowing our personal limits. Life offers many choices, and every person should select friends who match their own strength.

Let us consider the metal pot and the pot made from earth once more. If the weak pot had been made from metal, the journey would have ended differently. Both pots would have survived the rough water and reached the end of the stream. Instead, the weak material broke under the pressure of the heavy current. This event teaches us that different people have different abilities. No one should attempt tasks which are too difficult for their own body or mind. If you want to live a long and happy life, you must avoid bad danger. Wise individuals always look at the situation before they make a final choice. When strange problems appear, strong friends cannot always save you. Therefore, staying in a safe place with similar friends is much better. Parents and teachers often tell this tale to help children understand the world. They want every young person to grow up with good knowledge about risks. If you listen to these old stories, you will make better decisions in the future. The memory of the two pots remains a powerful lesson for all of us today.

### C1
Long ago, beside the winding banks of a great and restless river, two vessels shared a quiet dwelling. One was fashioned from fragile earthenware, shaped by human hands and baked under the soft glow of an open fire. The other was forged from stern, unyielding metal, heavy with the dense weight of its own substance. Though they lived in close proximity, their natures could not have been more distinct. The earthen pot possessed a delicate beauty, light and graceful, yet acutely vulnerable to the slightest blow. The metal vessel stood proud and dark, indifferent to the common hazards that troubled its fragile companion. One particularly bright morning, when the sunlight danced across the glassy surface of the water, the metal pot cast its gaze toward the flowing stream. It proposed a grand adventure, suggesting that they embark on a long journey down the current to see the distant lands. The earthen vessel felt a sudden tremor of hesitation ripple through its hollow core. It knew too well that water was both its cradle and its executioner, capable of shattering its fragile shell with a single careless impact. Yet the metal pot pressed the invitation with absolute confidence, offering a solemn promise of protection. It vowed to remain close at hand, shielding its companion from every hazard the river might present. Reassured by this steadfast pledge, the earthen pot allowed itself to be coaxed toward the sloping bank. Together, they slid into the cool embrace of the current, abandoning the safety of the shore for the unpredictable depths. At first, the journey was a gentle pastoral dream. The river carried them forward with a smooth, benevolent grace, their rounded bodies bobbing in gentle synchronization across the sunlit ripples. The earthen pot began to shed its initial dread, lulled into a false sense of security by the tranquil rhythm of the voyage. It marvelled at the shifting landscape of green reeds and overhanging branches, believing fully in the protective shadow of its metallic friend. Not far downstream, however, the character of the river began to shift with subtle menace. The banks drew closer together, squeezing the vast volume of water into a narrow, stone choked gorge where the current quickened its pace. The gentle drift transformed into a turbulent rush, churning white against jagged boulders that broke the surface like the teeth of a sleeping beast. Panic seized the earthen pot as the water battered its sides with escalating fury. The metal pot, realizing the sudden peril, attempted to swim closer to fulfill its generous promise. Yet the heavy, rushing water drove a wedge between them, pushing the companions apart with merciless indifference.

Caught in the swirling vortex, the light earthen vessel was hurled violently toward a sharp, protruding rock. Because its material was fundamentally frail, the sudden and brutal impact proved catastrophic. In a fraction of a second, the earthen pot fractured into a multitude of useless shards, scattering across the churning foam. The metal pot could do nothing to arrest the tragedy unfolding before its eyes. It fought desperately against the current, striving to reach the spot where its companion had vanished, but it was already far too late. Burdened by its own dense composition and overwhelmed by the force of the water, the metal pot sank deep into the dark, silent depths, disappearing forever from the light of day. This ancient misfortune has echoed through generations, serving as a solemn reminder that unequal companionships carry inherent perils. If an individual lacks the fortitude to withstand adversity, that person must exercise extreme caution regarding whom they choose to trust. A wise person recognizes personal limitations with clear vision, steadfastly avoiding dangerous situations born of vanity or misplaced ambition. True security is never found in the grand promises of others, but rather in a sober understanding of one own capacity. For centuries, elders have shared this narrative with the young, ensuring that the lesson is learned before the storms of life arrive. Many families across the country adopted this tale as a cornerstone of early instruction. If the metal pot had not initiated the journey, the earthen vessel would have remained undisturbed in its safety. Yet the fatal flaw lay in the blind trust placed upon a promise that could not withstand the reality of the river. We are thus reminded of the danger of attempting to mimic the strength of those who are fundamentally different from ourselves. Powerful friends, no matter how sincere their intentions, may inadvertently create ruin for those whose constitution is far more delicate. Every school curriculum and domestic circle has echoed this wisdom to temper youthful recklessness. Consider once more the distinct properties of the two vessels that entered the stream. If the weaker pot had been forged from the same enduring metal, the narrative would have reached a very different conclusion. Both companions would have braved the churning rapids, emerging victorious at the mouth of the great river. Instead, the inherent weakness of the material crumbled beneath the relentless pressure of the heavy current. This event illustrates the undeniable truth that different beings possess vastly different capabilities. No one should ever attempt tasks that exceed the natural limits of their mind or body.

To live a long and tranquil existence, one must actively avoid unnecessary hazards and alluring dangers. Prudent individuals invariably evaluate every circumstance with meticulous care before committing to a definitive path. When unexpected crises emerge, even the most loyal allies may prove powerless to intervene. Consequently, remaining within a sphere of safety alongside those who share one own level of strength is infinitely preferable. Parents and mentors continue to recount this parable to equip children with the discernment required for survival. They desire that every growing mind comprehends the delicate balance between aspiration and capacity. By listening attentively to these timeless traditions, individuals can navigate the treacherous currents of the future with greater wisdom. The memory of the two pots remains a potent symbol of consequence and reality. Life presents countless choices, demanding that every person selects companions who match their own physical and emotional fortitude. Those who fail to heed this guidance often find themselves overtaken by events they never anticipated. In the end, protection is not a gift bestowed by others, but an achievement rooted in self awareness.

## st-0251 — Androcles and the Lion
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1

### B1
Long ago, a poor man named Androcles lived in a country far away. His life was hard, and he ran away from his master. He walked for many days until he reached a wild, empty forest. ### B2
Long ago, a poor man named Androcles lived in a country far away. His life was very hard, and he had to run away from his master. He was treated badly by his master. He walked for many days. Finally, he reached a wild, empty forest. No other people lived in this forest. While he was walking through the green trees, he suddenly heard a large animal crying in pain. Androcles was afraid at first. Then he saw a huge animal on the ground. The poor creature could not stand up. A big point was stuck deep inside its foot. The animal looked at the man and made a soft sound. It was as if the animal was asking for help. Androcles stayed near the place because he wanted to understand the situation. He knew that the creature needed care. He looked around the forest to find some water. He brought water to the animal. The animal drank the water and felt slightly better. Androcles hoped that he could do more. The great giant watched him with large eyes. There was no doubt that the animal felt great pain. Androcles decided to approach the giant very slowly. He did not want to cause any trouble. He spoke in a soft voice to calm the creature down. The giant listened carefully to the human. It seemed that the animal understood the kind words. Androcles felt a deep sense of quiet. He knew that he must help his new friend. The forest was quiet around them. Only the wind moved through the leaves. Androcles prepared himself for the task ahead. He knew it might take some time. He was ready to spend hours if necessary. The animal made another soft sound. It was a clear sign of trust. The giant gave a loud cry of pain. However, the point came out of the foot at last. The animal felt much better immediately. It gave a happy little sound. From that day, the creature remembered the good act and stayed with his new human friend. The animal hunted food every day. It brought meat for Androcles to eat. They lived together in quiet for several months. They shared a quiet life in the deep forest. They spent many hours together under the green trees. The giant protected Androcles from other wild creatures. Androcles shared his food and water with the giant. They became very close friends over time. The man taught the animal many things about living in the woods. The animal learned how to find the best water. They walked together every morning. The sun shone brightly through the branches. Life in the forest was simple and good. They had no worries during those months. The animal was always happy to see Androcles. Androcles loved his animal friend dearly. They trusted each other without any doubt. The forest felt like a real home to them. Nothing could disturb their quiet days. They were safe among the tall trees. However, some soldiers eventually found Androcles. They took him back to the city as a prisoner. The journey back to the city was long and hard. Androcles felt very sad about leaving the forest. He missed his animal friend every single moment. The soldiers brought him to a dark room. He had to stay there until the final day. In those days, men who ran away were often brought to a public place. They were treated very badly there. They put Androcles into a large open area. There was a wild animal with him. This animal had not eaten for days. The crowd watched from their seats. They expected the poor man to die very quickly. The dangerous animal ran out toward the prisoner with a loud, frightening sound. When the animal came close, it suddenly stopped. It recognized the man. Instead of attacking him, the animal began to touch Androcles with its head. It was like a friendly dog. Everyone in the audience was very surprised to see such a strange sight. The people in the crowd whispered to each other. They could not believe what they were seeing. The ruler of the city stood up from his seat. He watched the strange event with great interest. He wanted to know how this was possible. The ruler of the city heard about this wonderful event. He called the prisoner to him. Androcles told the whole story about the point. He explained how they had lived together in the forest. The leader was so happy that he gave the man his freedom. He also let the great creature go back to the wild. Androcles thanked the ruler for his great kindness. The crowd shouted for the man and the giant. Everyone was happy that the story ended well. Androcles walked out of the city as a free person. The giant animal returned to the green forest. They never forgot the time they spent together.

### C1
In a remote province far from the marble and noise of the empire, a wretched man named Androcles endured a life of perpetual shadow. Bound to a cruel master whose temper was as volatile as a summer storm, Androcles found his existence reduced to endless toil and silent suffering. Driven by a desperate longing for dignity, he finally resolved to flee the heavy chains of his servitude under the cover of a moonless night. For many days he wandered blindly through unfamiliar terrain, his spirit bruised and his body weary from relentless flight. At last, seeking refuge from the relentless sun, he crossed the boundary into a wild, untouched forest where no human footstep had ever fallen.

While navigating the dense thicket of emerald leaves, he was startled by a low, agonizing moan that vibrated through the quiet air. Androcles froze, his heart hammering against his ribs as fear whispered warnings of predatory beasts. Cautiously peering past a curtain of ferns, he discovered a colossal lion collapsed upon the moss, its massive frame trembling with distress. The proud creature was entirely unable to rise, its breath coming in ragged gasps of pure torment. Upon closer inspection, Androcles saw that a massive wooden thorn was deeply embedded within the thick pad of the animal's paw.

Instead of fleeing the potential danger, the runaway felt a profound surge of empathy eclipse his initial terror. Knowing the creature desperately required relief, he searched the undergrowth until he found a clear stream, cupping cool water in his palms. He returned to the giant beast and offered the moisture, watching as the lion drank eagerly and relaxed its powerful muscles slightly. The great golden eyes locked onto Androcles, communicating a plea for mercy that transcended the barriers between species. Murmuring soothing, melodic phrases to calm the trembling giant, Androcles prepared himself for the delicate and perilous operation ahead.

With infinite patience, he seated himself beside the enormous limb, resting his bare hands against the coarse, tawny fur. The lion remained utterly still, granting its absolute trust to the fragile human who knelt before it. Taking a deep, steadying breath, Androcles grasped the jagged point and pulled with sudden, calculated firmness. A deafening roar of pain shook the canopy, yet the offending thorn slid free at last from the swollen flesh. Instantly relieved of its agony, the majestic beast let out a soft, contented purr and brushed its massive head affectionately against Androcles.

From that transformative morning onward, the lion refused to part from the man who had delivered it from suffering. Acting as both provider and guardian, the noble creature hunted diligently through the dense woods and brought fresh meat to their shared camp. They spent several peaceful months together beneath the verdant branches, establishing a harmonious rhythm far removed from civilization. Androcles shared his meager rations and taught the animal the safest paths to fresh springs, their bond deepening with every passing season. Enclosed by the whispering trees, they existed in a tranquil sanctuary where neither fear nor cruelty could reach them.

Alas, this idyllic isolation could not endure indefinitely against the relentless reach of imperial authority. Imperial soldiers, scouring the countryside for runaways, eventually tracked Androcles to his hidden forest haven. Torn mercilessly from his sanctuary, he was dragged back toward the bustling city in heavy irons, his heart weighed down by profound grief. The journey was long and dreary, filled with the bitter knowledge that he would never see his fierce companion again. Upon arrival, they cast him into a damp, windowless cell to await the public games where escaped slaves met their grim fates.

The arena was a vast, sunlit bowl of stone surrounded by thousands of baying spectators eager for bloodshed. Dragged into the center of the sandy floor, Androcles stood alone, waiting for the release of the starving beast that would end his life. A heavy iron gate groaned open across the yard, and a massive lion surged into the light with a terrifying roar. It was the very same creature from the forest, starved and desperate, charging fiercely toward the defenseless prisoner. The crowd leaned forward in breathless anticipation, waiting for the inevitable tragedy to unfold upon the crimson sand.

Yet, as the great beast closed the distance, its fierce momentum suddenly vanished into thin air. Recognizing the familiar scent and silhouette of its benefactor, the lion skidded to a halt mere paces away. Instead of tearing into the helpless man, the giant creature began to nuzzle Androcles with gentle affection, wagging its tail like a loyal puppy. A stunned silence fell over the amphitheater as the roaring monster dissolved into a picture of domestic devotion. Whispers of disbelief rippled through the staggered crowd while the ruler of the city stood up to observe the impossible scene.

Summoned before the ruler's elevated box, Androcles recounted the entire tale of the forest and the thorn with remarkable humility. He described how they had shared meals, shelter, and companionship through the long, quiet months under the green leaves. Moved profoundly by the loyalty between man and beast, the ruler declared that such a miracle deserved a greater reward than mere survival. With a wave of his royal hand, he granted Androcles his full freedom and ordered that the magnificent lion be returned safely to the wild. A tremendous cheer erupted from the assembled masses, celebrating a rare mercy that transcended the harsh laws of the empire.

Androcles stepped out of the grand arena gates as a completely free man, breathing the crisp air of liberty once more. The great lion loped beside him through the city streets before turning its gaze toward the distant, beckoning horizon of the green forest. With a final, lingering look of deep affection, the giant creature bounded away into its native home among the tall trees. Though their paths diverged that afternoon, neither man nor beast ever forgot the profound bond forged in the quiet heart of the wilderness. Androcles lived out his remaining years in peace, carrying the memory of that forest sanctuary like a precious treasure within his soul.

## st-0252 — The Monkey's Heart
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, one little person lives near water. He eats food and plays. A big person watches him every day. One day, the big person wants him. He speaks to the little person. He wants the little person to come. They go into the water. The big person stops.

He tells the little person something. He needs his life. The little person feels bad. He does not show it. He thinks of one plan. He says his life is up. The big person believes the story. They return to the land. The little person goes up.

Once, one little person lives near water. He eats food and plays. A big person watches him every day. One day, the big person wants him. He speaks to the little person. He wants the little person to come. They go into the water. The big person stops.

He tells the little person something. He needs his life. The little person feels bad. He does not show it. He thinks of one plan. He says his life is up. The big person believes the story. They return to the land. The little person goes up.

### A2
Once upon a time, a good little animal lived near a large water place. The water place was full of good food. Every single day, the animal ate fine food and played in high trees. A big animal lived in the same water. He watched the little animal every single morning. One fine afternoon, the big water animal wanted to catch the good little animal for food. He came close to the land. He spoke in a very good voice. The big water animal asked the little animal to go across the water on his back. The little animal believed the big animal. He went upon his back without any fear.

They moved away from the green land. They went deep into the middle of the water. Suddenly, the water became dark. The big animal stopped moving forward. The big animal looked back at the person on his back. He told him a bad plan. He said that he needed the little animal heart for a special food. The good little animal felt very worried. However, he did not show any fear on his face. In a fast way, the good little animal thought of a good plan to save his life. He told the big animal that his heart was not inside his body.

He explained that he always left his heart near the plant tree. The bad big animal believed the story. He turned his body back toward the land. As soon as they reached the land, the fast little animal went off. He went up the high tree in a good way. He laughed at the bad big animal. He stayed in the high tree and lived happily every single day. He never went back to the water again. The big animal waited near the land for a long time. He never saw the little animal again. That is the end of the story.

### B1
Once upon a time, a person lived near a large river full of sweet food. Every day, the person ate fresh fruit and played happily in the tall trees. A big animal lived in the same water and watched the little person every single morning. One warm afternoon, the big animal wanted to catch the person for dinner. He swam close to the land and spoke in a very friendly voice to the person. The big animal invited the person to ride across the water upon his broad back. The person trusted the big animal and jumped onto his back without any fear. They moved away from the green land and traveled deep into the middle of the river. Suddenly, the water became deep and dark, and the big animal stopped moving forward. The big animal looked back at the passenger and told him a terrible plan. He said that he needed the heart to make a special meal. The person felt very worried, but he did not show any feeling on his face. He needed to think of something right away to save his life. The water was very deep, and the big animal was strong. The person looked around the river and tried to stay calm. He knew that he could not fight the big animal in the water. He had to use his mind to find a good way out of this danger. The big animal waited for an answer and smiled a bad smile. The person looked at the big animal and began to speak with a calm voice. He told the big animal that his heart was not inside his body today. He explained that he always left his heart hanging upon the branches of the fruit tree. The big animal believed the story and turned his heavy body back toward the land. As soon as they reached the land, the person jumped off the animal. He climbed up the tall tree safely and laughed at the animal. Once, the person has lived near the river for a long time. If the big animal comes back, the person will climb up the tall tree again. The person has eaten fresh fruit every single day since he moved to the trees. If the animal wants dinner, he will never catch the person again. The person has understood the terrible plan since the middle of the river. If the animal stays in the water, the person will feel safe. The big animal waited under the tree for a long time. He hoped that the person would come down from the high branches. However, the person stayed in the tree and looked down at the water. The animal realized that he was not able to catch his dinner. He turned around and swam away into the dark water. The person lived happily in the green trees every single day. He has learned a very important lesson about trust and danger. If another animal speaks to him, he will not trust the voice. The river flows past the trees, and the sweet food remains available. Life in the high branches is good for the little person. He has enjoyed many peaceful days since that strange afternoon. If anyone asks about the journey, the person tells the true story. The big animal has never returned to bother the person again. The person has kept his heart safe within his own body. Every morning brings new fruit and a quiet place to play. If the sun shines warmly, the person sleeps in the shade. The story of the river and the animal lives on in the trees. If you visit the river, you might see the tall fruit trees. The person has found happiness and peace in his home. He will always remember the day he crossed the deep water.

### B2
Once upon a time, a fast animal lived near a large river full of sweet food. Every day, the animal ate fresh fruit and played happily in the tall trees. A big water creature lived in the same water and watched the little creature every single morning. One warm afternoon, the water creature wanted to catch the fast animal for dinner. He swam close to the land and spoke in a very friendly voice to the creature. The water creature invited the animal to ride across the water upon his broad back. The animal trusted the big creature and jumped onto his back without any fear. They moved away from the green shore and traveled deep into the middle of the river. Suddenly, the water became deep and dark, and the water creature stopped moving forward. The water creature looked back at the passenger and told him a terrible plan. He said that he needed the heart of the animal to make a special meal. The fast animal felt very worried, but he did not show any panic on his face. Quickly, the animal thought of a brilliant trick to save his life. He told the water creature that his heart was not inside his body today. He explained that he always left his heart hanging upon the branches of the fruit tree. The silly creature believed the story and turned his heavy body back toward the land. As soon as they reached the shore, the quick animal jumped off the creature. He climbed up the tall tree safely and laughed loudly at the silly creature. The animal was safe because he had used his mind during a dangerous moment. He would never trust another water creature again for the rest of his life.

Many years ago, the river was quiet and calm. The sun shone brightly upon the green trees and the cool water. The animal spent hours gathering food and enjoying the lovely weather. He knew that other creatures lived in the river, but he usually did not worry about them. However, the large water creature had observed the animal for a long period. The creature felt empty inside and decided that a fine meal would be wonderful. He planned his approach carefully before he spoke to the innocent animal. If the animal had known the truth, he would have run away immediately. Instead, the friendly words of the creature made the animal feel completely safe. He accepted the invitation because he wanted to see the other side of the river. The journey began nicely, but the mood changed as the water grew darker. When the creature stopped in the middle, the terrible truth was finally revealed. The animal understood that his life was in serious danger. He had to think of something smart if he wanted to survive. His quick mind helped him invent a story about his heart. The creature was stupid enough to believe every single word. They turned around and swam back toward the familiar land. The moment they touched the shore, the smart animal escaped into the branches. He looked down at the disappointed creature and smiled with relief. That experience taught him an important lesson about trust.

Life by the river continued quietly after that strange afternoon. The animal always remembered the danger he had faced on the water. He never forgot how easily he could have lost his life. Whenever he saw the water creature swimming nearby, he stayed high up in the trees. The creature realized that he had been tricked by a much smaller animal. He felt stupid for believing that a heart could be left on a branch. From that day forward, the creature stayed in the deep water and left the animal alone. The animal shared his story with other creatures living near the forest. Everyone agreed that intelligence was much more powerful than physical strength. If anyone ever faced a similar problem, they should try to use their mind. Preparation and quick thinking could save anyone from a difficult situation. The river remained a place where both friends and enemies crossed paths. Yet, the smart animal was always prepared for any bad trouble. He ate his sweet fruit every morning and enjoyed the beauty of nature. Nothing could disturb his happiness now that he was truly wise. He looked across the wide river and felt grateful for his smart escape. The memory of the event faded slowly as the seasons changed. Years passed, but the story was told again and again by the river. It became a famous tale among all the animals in the local community. And so, the smart animal lived happily for the rest of his days.

### C1
Many years ago, the great river flowed through a landscape of perpetual green, quiet and heavy with the scent of damp earth. The sun shone brightly upon the lush trees and the cool, emerald water that offered endless nourishment to those who lived nearby. In this peaceful haven lived a remarkably swift animal, whose days were spent gathering sweet fruit and leaping through the canopy. He knew that other creatures inhabited the river, but he rarely gave them a second thought. Beneath the surface, however, a massive water creature had observed the animal for a long period. Watching the agile arboreal resident every single morning, the creature felt a strange emptiness inside his massive frame. He decided that a fine meal made from the animal would be a wonderful luxury to break the monotony of his days. Slowly and deliberately, he planned his approach before making any attempt to speak to his unsuspecting prey.

One particularly warm afternoon, the water creature decided that the moment for action had finally arrived. He swam close to the muddy bank, adjusting his massive bulk to appear as harmless as possible. Speaking in a remarkably warm and friendly voice, he greeted the animal with unexpected charm. Had the animal known the truth of the hidden intentions, he would have fled into the deep forest immediately. Instead, the soothing words made him feel entirely secure in the presence of the stranger. The water creature extended a gracious invitation for the animal to ride across the river upon his broad back. He claimed there were even sweeter fruits on the opposite shore waiting to be discovered. The trusting animal, feeling no fear whatsoever, accepted the offer without hesitation and jumped onto the large back. He settled comfortably into the rough scales, imagining a pleasant afternoon adventure across the wide expanse of water. They moved away from the green shore, gliding smoothly as they traveled deep into the middle of the river.

Suddenly, the friendly atmosphere vanished as the water grew remarkably deep, dark, and menacing. The heavy creature stopped moving forward entirely, treading water in the middle of the vast river. The animal felt a sudden chill run through his veins as the true horror of the situation became apparent. Looking back at his terrified passenger, the water creature revealed a terribly cruel plan. He explained in a cold tone that he needed the heart of the animal to prepare a special meal. The fast animal experienced a wave of intense worry, yet he managed to keep his composure completely hidden. He knew that showing panic would seal his fate and end his life right there in the dark water. Quickly and calmly, his brilliant mind began searching for a way out of this deadly trap. He realized that physical strength was useless here, leaving only his wits to save him. Without a single tremor in his voice, he prepared to deliver a masterclass in deception.

Addressing the giant creature with feigned casualness, the animal claimed that his heart was not actually inside his body today. He explained with absolute seriousness that he always left his vital organ hanging safely upon the branches of the fruit tree. He expressed deep regret that he had not brought it along for the journey across the water. Had he known about the grand dinner plans, he would have retrieved it before leaving the shore. The foolish water creature listened closely, completely captivated by the bizarre explanation. The heavy creature was naive enough to believe every single word spoken by his clever passenger. He worried that a heart left behind on a tree might be damaged, ruining his long awaited meal. Without questioning the logic of such an absurd biological claim, he turned his massive body around. They began swimming urgently back toward the familiar land, cutting through the dark water with renewed purpose. The animal maintained his outward calm, watching the shoreline grow closer with every powerful stroke.

As soon as their heavy form touched the muddy bank, the quick animal wasted no time. With a burst of explosive energy, he leaped off the creature and scrambled up the trunk of a tall tree. He climbed swiftly until he reached the safety of the upper branches, far out of reach of the water. Looking down at the disappointed giant, the animal smiled with profound relief. He let out a loud, mocking laugh that echoed across the quiet surface of the river. The water creature realized instantly that he had been completely outsmarted by a much smaller being. He felt intensely foolish for believing that any living creature could leave its heart upon a branch. From that day forward, the chastised giant remained strictly in the deep water and left the tree dweller alone. The animal was finally safe, having used his quick thinking during a moment of extreme peril. He vowed never to trust another resident of the river for the rest of his days.

Life by the great river continued quietly after that strange and terrifying afternoon. The animal always remembered the profound danger he had faced out on the dark water. He never forgot how easily he could have lost his life due to misplaced trust. Whenever he saw the water creature swimming nearby, he simply stayed high up in the protective branches. The memory of the event served as a permanent reminder of the fragility of existence. The clever animal soon shared his incredible story with the other creatures living near the forest. Everyone who heard the tale agreed unanimously that intelligence was far more powerful than physical strength. They discussed how preparation and quick thinking could save anyone from a difficult situation. The river remained a complex place where both friends and natural enemies constantly crossed paths. Yet, the smart animal was always thoroughly prepared for any bad trouble that might arise.

He ate his sweet fruit every single morning and thoroughly enjoyed the serene beauty of the natural world. Nothing could disturb his profound happiness now that he felt truly wise and experienced. Looking across the wide river, he felt an overwhelming sense of gratitude for his clever escape. The memory of the terrifying event faded slowly as the changing seasons transformed the landscape. Years passed quietly by the water, turning the remarkable event into local folklore. The story was told again and again by the older animals to the younger generations in the community. It became a famous legend among all the creatures who made their homes near the forest canopy. They learned that caution and mental agility were the greatest tools for survival in a dangerous world. And so, the smart animal lived happily and peacefully for the rest of his long days. His legacy endured as a testament to the triumph of wit over brute force.
