## st-0264 — The Steadfast Tin Soldier
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A man makes twenty little figures. He makes them from a hard thing. The last figure has only one part. He has only one foot. He stands as well as others. He stays on a table all day. A paper girl stands near him. She has only one part too. She has only one foot. The man looks at her all day. He wants her for his love. He thinks about her every morning. He thinks about her every night. A black person becomes very bad. He makes bad work for man. The man falls down from room. He lands right on the way. Two small children put him down. They put him in a house. The house is made of paper.

The house goes into the water. Water covers the little man. He stays under water for time. He stays for a long time. A big fish comes along now. A large fish eats the man. The fish goes through water. A woman cuts the fish open. She finds the little man right there. The man sees the paper girl again. He is very happy to see her. A child takes the man up. He puts the man into the light. The light is very big. They stay together in the light. They become one hard thing now. Their love stays true all time.

### A2
Once upon a time, a group of figures was made. A man had only enough matter for the small people. Every single figure stood firm. The last one had only one foot because the matter ran out. He stood as well as the others. The man made each figure with care. He put them all on a big table.

On the table, there was a beautiful paper house. A lovely little paper dancer stood there too. She raised her arms on one foot. The small figure thought she was just like him. He watched her all day long. He wanted to make her his wife. He looked at her again and again.

Night came very soon. A black person sat inside a small box. The person became bad with the figure. He told him to look away from her. The figure acted as if he did not hear him. The next morning, the poor figure fell down. He lay on the cold floor.

Two young boys found him outside in the cold state. They placed him into a small paper house. The house moved down a dark water line. A large fish came up in the dark water. The animal swallowed the tin soldier in the canal. The figure had no pass. The house moved faster into a wide place with water. Water covered him completely.

A large fish came up. It took the figure whole in the dark water. Some time passed by. A woman caught the fish and bought it. She cut the fish open. She found the figure inside. She carried him back to the room.

The figure was very happy. He saw his lovely paper dancer standing again. A small child picked up the figure. He threw him right into the hot fire. A strong wind moved the paper dancer too. They died together. Nothing remained except a small heart made of the same matter.

### B1
Once upon a time, a group of metal figures was made in a factory. The man had only enough metal for twenty-five figures. Every single soldier stood straight on the table. The last one had only one leg. Although he had just one leg, he stood as firmly as the others. On the table, there was a paper house and a lovely paper dancer. The dancer stood on one leg while she raised her arms high. The metal soldier thought that she was just like him. He watched her all day because he wanted to make her his wife. When night came, a black toy character became angry with the soldier. The character told him to look away from the dancer. The soldier acted like he did not hear him at all. The next morning, the poor soldier fell down from the window straight onto the street below.

Two young boys found him outside in the rain. They placed him into a small paper boat. The boat moved down a dark water path where a big animal demanded a ticket. Because the soldier had no ticket, the boat moved into a wide area of water. The water covered him completely. Suddenly, a large fish swam up and took the soldier whole. After some time, the fish was caught and bought by a cook. When the cook cut the fish open, she found the soldier inside. She carried him back to the same room. The soldier was very happy to see his lovely paper dancer again. Suddenly, a small child picked up the soldier and threw him into the warm fire. A strong wind blew the paper dancer into the fire too. They burned together until nothing remained except a small heart made of metal.

Many days have passed since the fire burned the little metal soldier. If someone looks closely at the ashes, they will see that small metal heart. The metal heart has stayed there for a long time. People have talked about the brave soldier and the paper dancer. Their story has lived on in this room. The small house and the table are still part of the room. Children have played games near the window where the soldier fell. No one has forgotten the little toy with one leg. Every afternoon, the light shines across the floor. The memory of the dancer and the soldier remains strong. We have remembered their love through all these years.

### B2
Once upon a time, a group of strong metal soldiers was made by a man. The man had only enough metal for twenty figures. Every single soldier stood straight. The last one had only one leg because the metal ran out. Although he had just one leg, he stood as firmly as the others who had two. On the table where they lived, there was a wonderful paper castle and a lovely little paper dancer. The dancer stood on one leg. She raised her arms. This made the metal soldier think that she was just like him. He watched her all day because he wanted to make her his wife. When night came, a black toy enemy that sat inside a small box became angry with the soldier. The enemy told him to look away from the dancer. The soldier pretended that he did not hear. The next morning, the poor soldier fell down from the window straight onto the street below.

Two young boys found him outside in the rain. They placed him into a small paper boat. The boat sailed down a dark water pipe. A big rat demanded to see a pass. Because the soldier had no pass, the boat rushed faster into a wide water path. The water covered him completely. Suddenly, a large fish swam up and took the soldier whole in the dark water. After some time had passed, the fish was caught and taken to a market. A cook bought the fish. When the cook cut the fish open, she found the soldier inside. She carried him back to the same room. The soldier was very happy to see his lovely paper dancer standing again in her place.

Suddenly, a small child picked up the metal soldier and threw him straight into the warm fire. A strong wind blew the paper dancer into the fire too. They burned together until nothing remained except a small heart made of metal. The fire burned very hot. The room was filled with bright light. The small heart remained where the fire had been. People who saw the metal heart knew that the story was true. The metal heart stayed there for a long time. Every person who entered the room could see the small heart. The memory of the soldier and the dancer lived on. Their love was stronger than the fire. Nothing could change what they felt for each other. The end of the story came with peace.

### C1
Once upon a time, from a worn iron spoon cast aside in a dusty workshop, twenty magnificent brothers were born into the world. Their creator had only enough precious metal for that exact number, shaping each infantryman with proud shoulders and a steady gaze. Every single soldier stood straight in his polished uniform, bearing the weight of his duty without a single complaint. The very last of them, however, had been fashioned with only one leg because the molten tin ran out before his mold could be fully satisfied. Although he balanced on a single limb, he stood as firmly and resolutely as the others who possessed a matched pair. They were placed upon a wooden table amidst a glittering array of discarded playthings and forgotten treasures. Beside them stood a wonderful paper castle with tiny glass windows reflecting the morning light, and near its gates lingered a lovely little paper dancer. She wore a skirt of gossamer gauze and a narrow blue ribbon across her shoulder, pinned with a sparkling sequin. The dancer stood entirely on one leg, extending her other limb backward so high that the observant metal soldier could not see it at all. This elegant posture made the lonely tin soldier think that she was just like him, bearing her singular balance with grace. He watched her across the varnished wood all day long, wishing to make her his wife, though he dared not speak his mind aloud. Not once did he regret his missing limb, for he felt that they shared a silent understanding that transcended ordinary speech. When night descended upon the quiet room, the toys were freed from their boxes to play at housekeeping and war. A small, black toy enemy that sat snugly inside a painted wooden box suddenly became intensely jealous of the soldier. The dark jack-in-the-box told him sharply to look away from the dancer and mind his own station. The soldier pretended that he did not hear the warning, keeping his gaze fixed firmly on the delicate lady of paper. The goblin grinned maliciously and hissed that the morning would bring a reckoning for such stubborn disregard of authority. The next morning, as the first pale rays of dawn filtered through the dusty panes, the poor soldier fell down from the window. Whether the malicious goblin pushed him or the autumn wind caught his heavy base remains a mystery, but he tumbled straight onto the cobblestone street below.

The descent was swift and terrifying, landing the soldier headfirst between the paving stones with his bayonet stuck firmly in the mud. Two young boys, running past in the damp morning air, found him immediately and paused to inspect their prize. They placed him carefully into a small paper boat folded from a discarded newspaper and pushed him out into the gutter. The street had become a rushing river from the overnight rain, and the frail vessel sailed down a dark water pipe with alarming speed. Darkness closed in around the little traveler as the paper walls began to soften under the relentless assault of the swirling current. A large water rat that lived beneath the wooden planks of the drain suddenly demanded to see a pass. Because the soldier had no passport or official papers to show, the terrified boat rushed faster into a wide water path. The dirty water covered him completely, threatening to sweep him away into the murky depths of the underground pipes. Suddenly, a large and hungry fish swam up from the shadows and took the soldier whole in the dark water. Inside the belly of the beast, it was pitch black and terribly cramped, yet the soldier never dropped his rifle. After some time had passed in that suffocating darkness, a fisherman caught the creature in a great net and took it to the busy market. A cook bought the fish for the midday meal and carried it back to the warm kitchen with a heavy basket. When the cook cut the fish open with a sharp knife, she was astonished to find the soldier inside the glistening belly. She picked him up by his belt with two fingers and carried him back to the very same room where he had lived before. The soldier was remarkably unchanged by his ordeal, save for a slight tarnish on his bright red coat. He was exceedingly happy to see his lovely paper dancer standing once again in her familiar place by the castle.

Suddenly, without any warning, a small child picked up the metal soldier and threw him straight into the warm fire of the tiled stove. The child gave no reason for the cruel act, acting out of a sudden, careless impulse. A strong draft rushed through the room and blew the paper dancer off her platform and into the fire too. They burned together in the flickering flames until nothing remained of their paper and paint except a small heart made of solid metal. The fire burned very hot, fed by dry pine logs that crackled and spat sparks into the dark hearth. The room was filled with a bright, blinding light as the intense heat melted the tin soldier down to a shapeless lump. Yet the small heart remained intact where the fire had been the fiercest, glowing with a soft, enduring warmth. People who later cleared the cold ashes and saw the metal heart knew that the strange story was true. The metal heart stayed there upon the iron grate for a long time, untouched by the sweeping brush or the gathering dust. Every person who entered the room could see the small heart resting among the gray cinders. The memory of the steadfast soldier and the graceful dancer lived on in the hearts of those who looked upon it. Their enduring love proved to be stronger than the fire itself, surviving every trial the world could invent. Nothing could ever change what they felt for each other through all the trials of their brief existence. The end of the story came with a quiet peace that settled over the silent, shadowy room. The little metal heart rested softly in the ashes, carrying the quiet proof of a devotion that defied the flames.

## st-0265 — Snow-White and Rose-Red
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Two young girls live in a house. Their names are Snow-White and Rose-Red. They love their mother very much. Snow-White is very still. Rose-Red likes to play outside.

The year turns. A big black bear comes. He comes to the door. He wants a place to stay. The girls do not run away. The bear is good. He sits near a light. He stays there every day.

The year turns again. The bear must leave the house. He goes away to a big place. The girls feel bad. Later, they walk. They find a little man. He is in a problem.

The little man has a long face part. His head is in a tree. The girls help him. He is not good to them. He is bad. They see him again soon.

The bad man look at a bear. It is their friend, the big bear. The bear moves the bad man. The bad man run away. The bear become a man. He is a good prince.

The bad man take money. The prince tell the girls. Snow-White love the prince. Rose-Red love his family. They live in a large house. They give money to mother.

### A2
Two young girls lived near a deep forest. One girl was Snow-White. The other girl was Rose-Red. Snow-White was nice and good. Rose-Red loved to run and play outside. They loved their mother very much. The family lived in a small house. They had food to eat and beds to sleep. Life was good during most of the year. The sisters liked to help their mother. They cleaned the rooms every day. Then they went to play in the trees.

During cold months, a big black bear came. The animal walked up to the house. The big bear wanted to find a fire. At first, the children felt some fear. Soon, they let him sleep near the fire. The bear stayed with them for many days. The girls were nice to the large animal. He stayed until the air became hot. Then the bear had to leave the house. The sisters felt very bad when he left. They did not see the bear for weeks.

Weeks later, they found a small man. This man had a very long beard. His hair was caught in a tree. The sisters helped the man get free. He always spoke with much bad feeling. One day, they saw him make sad sounds. He was talking at a large animal. That animal was their old friend, the bear. The bear hit the mean man hard. The bad man ran away from them. Then the bear changed into a man.

The man told them a long story. He spoke about his lost money. Snow-White married the man. Rose-Red married the brother of the man. They lived in a huge house. Their mother lived with them too. Everyone was happy at the end.

### B1
Two young sisters named Snow-White and Rose-Red lived in a small house. They lived near a deep forest. Snow-White was quiet and good. Rose-Red liked to run and play outside. They were good children. They loved their mother very much. Every single day, the sisters helped around the house. They cleaned the floors and prepared nice food for their family. The mother always thanked them for their hard work and support. Life was calm and quiet in the small home near the trees.

During the cold winter months, a big black bear often came to their door. He came to seek rest from the freezing weather outside. At first, the girls were afraid of him. The kind animal showed that he meant no trouble. They let him come inside. He slept near the fire every night. The sisters gave him food and water. They felt safe with their large new friend. The bear stayed warm until the snow began to pass away. He never hurt anyone in the house.

When spring arrived and the weather became warm, the bear had to leave. He went away to protect his gold from a bad man. The sisters were very sad to see their animal friend go into the woods. They had shared many wonderful moments with him during the winter months. If the bear returns, they will welcome him back with open arms. They have missed him every single day since he left. The forest felt empty without his big presence near the door. Spring flowers grew around the house, but the girls still felt sad.

A few weeks later, while they were walking, they found a small man. The man had a long beard. This mean little man was in trouble because his hair was caught in a tree. The two sisters helped him several times. He was never nice to them. He always spoke with a strong voice and made angry sounds. The girls still helped him because they were good children. The small man showed no happiness for their help. He walked away and left them alone in the deep woods.

One day, they saw the same bad man shouting at a large animal. That animal was actually their old friend, the big bear. The bear had finally returned to catch the bad man. The bear hit the mean man hard until he ran away into the forest. Then the bear changed into a rich man because a spell had been broken. The man told them that the bad man had stolen his gold. He had also suffered a spell for a very long time. The sisters were amazed by this big change. They recognized their old friend immediately.

Snow-White later married the happy man. Rose-Red married his brother. They lived together in a huge house near the deep forest. They shared all their gold with their mother. If they see the poor man again, they will help him. They have lived a happy life since the spell ended. Their mother has enjoyed living with them in the big house. The two families have shared many wonderful meals together every week. Everyone in the area knows their story and remembers the big bear.

### B2
Two young sisters lived near a deep forest. They were named Snow-White and Rose-Red. Snow-White was quiet and gentle. Rose-Red liked to run and play outside. They were good children who loved their mother very much. During the cold winter months, a big black animal often came to their door to seek comfort. At first, the girls were afraid of him. The good creature showed that he meant no harm. They let him come inside and sleep near the fire every night. When spring arrived and the weather became nice, the bear had to leave. He needed to protect his gold from a bad man. The sisters were very sad to see their soft friend go away into the woods.

A few weeks later, while they were walking, they found a small man with a long beard who was in trouble. This mean little man was always stuck because his hair or coat was caught in different places. The two sisters helped him several times. However, he was never nice to them and always spoke with anger. One day, they saw the same bad man shouting at a large animal that was about to attack him. That animal was actually their old friend, the big bear, who had finally returned to catch the bad man. The bear hit the mean man hard until he stopped moving and ran away into the forest. Then the bear changed into a rich royal man because a spell had been broken. The royal man told them that the bad man had stolen his gold and forced him to live as a wild animal. Snow-White later married the happy royal man, and Rose-Red married his brother. They lived together in a huge castle and shared all their gold with their mother. Every single day brought new joy to the family. The mother felt very proud of her daughters.

They spent many hours walking in the large garden near the river. Sometimes the brothers joined them for a pleasant meal in the evening. The sun shone brightly over the tall trees of the forest. No danger could ever reach them inside their secure home. People from the nearby town often visited the castle to see the happy couple. The sisters always welcomed guests with a big smile. Life in the castle was quiet and full of light. They never forgot the cold winter days when the big black bear used to sleep by their fire. That strange friendship had changed everything for the better. Now they had everything they could possibly want in the world.

The memory of the little man with the long beard became just a funny story that they told at dinner. They laughed whenever they thought about how angry he always looked. The gold remained safe inside the strong walls of the castle. Nothing could disturb their quiet happiness anymore. The two sisters stayed together and cared for their mother until she grew old. Their children played happily in the great halls and learned many useful things. The whole country knew about the good heart of Snow-White and Rose-Red. Their names were remembered for generations across the land. Even today, people talk about the wonderful sisters who lived near the deep forest. The story ends with peace and love ruling over everyone in the royal house.

### C1
At the edge of a great and ancient forest stood a modest cottage where two sisters made their home. Their names were Snow-White and Rose-Red, matching the twin rose bushes that blossomed outside their window. Snow-White was of a quiet and gentle disposition, preferring the indoor warmth of books and spinning. Rose-Red, by contrast, possessed an ardent spirit that drew her out into the meadows to run and play. Despite their contrasting temperaments, they were devoted daughters who shared a deep affection for their widowed mother. Together, the three of them maintained a peaceful household sheltered by the whispering pines. As the bitter winds of winter descended upon the land, the forest grew menacing and silent. During these freezing months, a massive black bear frequently emerged from the deep woods to seek refuge at their door. At first, the girls recoiled in terror at the sight of such a formidable beast. Yet the creature soon demonstrated that he harbored no malicious intent toward them. Recognizing his gentle demeanor, they unlatched the heavy wooden door and welcomed him inside. Every evening, the bear stretched his heavy frame near the hearth, basking in the glow of the friendly fire. The sisters quickly lost their initial apprehension and treated the animal with genuine kindness. They would brush the snow from his thick fur and gently play with his rounded ears. The bear, for his part, accepted their affectionate ministrations with deep, rumbling contentment. When spring finally arrived and the harsh frost melted into sweet green grass, the bear had to depart. He explained to the bewildered household that he needed to venture deep into the woods to protect his gold from a wicked dwarf. Though they understood his urgent mission, the sisters felt a profound sadness as their noble friend vanished among the trees.

A few weeks after the bear's departure, the sisters ventured into the forest to gather dry wood for their hearth. While walking along a narrow path, they heard a sharp, complaining voice echoing through the undergrowth. Investigating the sound, they discovered a small man with an extraordinarily long, snowy beard. This mean-spirited little fellow was caught tightly in various obstacles, completely unable to free himself. First his beard was trapped in a fallen branch, and later his fine coat was snagged on a thorn bush. Each time they encountered him struggling, the two sisters immediately stepped forward to offer their assistance. However, gratitude was entirely foreign to the disposition of the ungracious little man. Instead of thanking the girls for their timely rescue, he invariably spoke to them with bitter anger and insults. He even snatched a piece of his own beard away from them rather than accept their gentle hands. Snow-White and Rose-Red simply shook their heads at his foolish pride and continued on their way. They had been raised to help those in distress, regardless of whether the recipient deserved their charity. Yet they could not help feeling relieved whenever the unpleasant creature finally hobbled away out of sight.

Fate brought the abrasive dwarf back into their lives on a bright morning later that season. While walking near the riverbank, the sisters caught sight of the familiar, angry little figure shouting furiously. He was standing near a large boulder, gesturing wildly at a massive animal that was about to attack him. That menacing creature was actually their old friend, the big black bear, who had finally returned from his arduous quest. The bear had tracked the wicked dwarf across the forest to recover the stolen treasure. Without a moment of hesitation, the great beast lunged forward and struck the mean man hard. The dwarf cried out in terror as the powerful blows rained down upon him from the furious bear. Realizing he could no longer escape his fate, he begged for mercy and promised to leave the forest forever. The bear gave him one final warning before the trembling creature turned and ran away into the dense woods. As the dust settled and the forest fell quiet, a remarkable transformation began to unfold before the astonished sisters. The great black bear suddenly cast off his animal skin and stood before them as a tall, handsome royal man. The heavy curse that had bound him to a beastly existence was finally broken forever.

The newly revealed prince explained the full truth of his long ordeal to the grateful family. He told them how the wicked dwarf had stolen his vast treasury and cast a cruel spell upon him. Only the defeat of the thief could break the enchantment and restore him to his rightful human form. Soon after these extraordinary events, Snow-White was wed to the royal man in a joyous ceremony that united their hearts. Her lively sister, Rose-Red, was married to the prince's loyal and kind-hearted brother shortly thereafter. The two couples decided to live together in a magnificent castle that overlooked the winding river and the dark forest. Generosity guided every aspect of their new life within the stone walls of the grand estate. They brought their beloved mother to live with them, ensuring she would never know hardship again. The vast gold that had been recovered from the dwarf was shared freely with the people of the land. Every single day brought new joy and laughter to the members of the united royal household. The mother felt an immense swell of pride whenever she looked upon her accomplished daughters. They spent countless serene hours walking through the vast garden while the sun shone brightly over the tall trees.

Evening meals were frequently shared with guests from the nearby town who traveled to witness the family's happiness. The sisters always welcomed visitors with a warm smile and a gracious demeanor that charmed everyone. Life inside the castle remained tranquil, anchored by a deep sense of security and enduring love. No shadow of danger could ever reach them behind the strong gates and the loyal guards. They often reminisced about the freezing winter days when the big black bear used to sleep peacefully by their fire. That strange and unlikely friendship had fundamentally altered the course of their lives for the better. The memory of the irritable little man with the long beard eventually faded into a mere dinner table anecdote. Whenever they recalled his frantic expressions and ungrateful words, the sisters could not help but laugh heartily. The recovered gold remained securely locked within the deep vaults of the castle, untouched by greed. Nothing could ever disturb the profound peace that had settled over their daily routines and quiet moments. The two sisters remained close companions, ensuring their mother was cared for with tenderness until her final days. Their children grew up strong and joyful within the great halls, learning the values of kindness and courage.

The entire country came to revere the noble heart and gentle spirit of Snow-White and Rose-Red. Their names were passed down through generations as a symbol of steadfast loyalty and compassion. Even years later, travelers walking near the deep forest would speak in hushed, affectionate tones about the wonderful sisters. The legacy of their remarkable journey lived on in the songs and stories told around winter hearths across the land. Peace and love reigned supreme within the royal house, governing every decision and every interaction. Thus, the sisters lived out their days in uninterrupted harmony, surrounded by the family they cherished so deeply.

## st-0266 — The Twelve Dancing Princesses
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
There is a man. He has twelve children. They stay in one big room. The man close the big door. Every morning, their shoes look bad. Nobody know why this is true.

The man make a plan. Men try to find the answer. They always fall down. A good man hear about this. He decide to try.

An old woman give him a top. The top make him not seen. The man have a room. The oldest girl bring food. The good man do not eat it.

He makes a face like he is down. The girls get up. They make a good sound. They open a door. They walk down into the land. The man follow them.

He put a hand on a thing. They reach a forest. The man take a white part of it.

They reach water. Twelve men wait in a group. They go to a house. Music play. Everyone play and move. Their shoes become bad by morning.

The men bring them back. The girls run to their room. The man follow them. He tells the man everything. He shows the white part of the forest.

The man ask the twelve children. They say yes to the plan. The good man choose the oldest girl. He becomes family. Everyone live together.

### A2
Once there was a leader with twelve lovely daughters. They lived together in one large room every night. The man always closed their big door well before bed. Every morning, their clothes were completely worn through. Nobody understood how that different thing happened. The door remained closed all night long.

The leader made a public call to the country. He promised that any man could marry one daughter. Many young men tried to stay outside. They always fell fast. A strong soldier heard about this difficult challenge. He decided to try his good time.

On his way, an old woman gave him a long dress. She told him that it would make him not seen. The leader welcomed him nice at the big house. He gave the soldier a room near the daughters. At night, the oldest daughter brought cold drink. The person did not drink the drink.

He just acted to fall fast instead. The twelve daughters got up and laughed. The young daughter thought he was sleeping deep. They opened a close door under their bed. They walked down into the dark ground.

Wearing his special dress, the soldier followed them. He touched a dress by chance. Her sister felt quite worried about that. Soon they reached a forest of white. The soldier broke off a small part.

They walked until they reached water. Twelve fine men waited there. Each daughter crossed the water with one man. They went to a house. Beautiful music played while everyone danced all night. The daughters danced until their clothes were worn through.

Before morning, the men brought them back. The daughters ran fast to their room. The soldier followed them close and got into bed. The next morning, the leader questioned him. The soldier told him everything he saw. He showed the part to the leader.

The leader called his twelve daughters. He asked them if the soldier was right. The daughters told everything to him. The strong soldier chose the oldest daughter. He became part of the family. Everyone lived happy together in the great house.

### B1
There once was a king. He had twelve lovely daughters. They lived in one room together. Every single night, the king locked their door. He locked it securely before he went to rest. However, when morning came, their shoes were always worn through completely. Nobody could understand how that strange thing happened. The door remained locked all night long.

The king made a public statement to the country. He promised a great reward. Any man who solved this problem might choose one daughter to be his wife. Many young men tried to stay up late outside the bedroom door. They always fell fast down in deep sleep. A soldier who had been fighting in wars heard about this challenge. He decided to try his luck.

On his way to the large house, an old woman gave him a special cover. She told him that the cover would make him hidden. She said he would be completely hidden during any time he wore it. When the soldier arrived, the king welcomed him nicely. The king gave him a room near the daughters. At night, the oldest daughter brought him a cup of sweet wine.

The smart soldier did not drink the wine. He acted like he fell fast down in deep sleep instead. Soon, the twelve daughters got up. They put on lovely clothes and laughed quietly. The youngest daughter said that the soldier was sleeping deeply. She said he would never open his eyes. They opened a secret door under their bed. They walked down into the dark earth.

Wearing his special cover, the soldier followed them closely down the hidden steps. At one point, he touched a daughter dress. This made her sister feel quite worried. Soon they arrived at a wonderful forest. All the leaves were made of gold and metal. The soldier broke off a small metal branch to keep as evidence of their journey.

They continued walking until they reached a broad river. Twelve other men waited in boats. Each daughter got into a boat with one man. They crossed the water to a bright house. Inside the bright house, beautiful music played. Everyone danced happily all night long. The daughters danced until their shoes were completely broken by morning.

Before the sun rose, the men brought them back across the river. The daughters ran quickly to their room. The soldier followed them closely. He got back into his bed just before they opened their eyes. When the king questioned him the next morning, the soldier told him everything. He showed the king the metal branch. He explained the secret night dance.

The king called his twelve daughters. He asked them if the soldier was telling the truth. Realizing that their secret plan was completely discovered, the daughters admitted everything. The soldier chose the oldest daughter to be his lovely wife. He became part of the family. From that day on, everyone lived happily together in the great house.

### B2
There once was a king who had twelve beautiful daughters. The daughters stayed in one large room together. Every single night, the king locked their heavy door securely before he went to rest. However, when morning came, their dancing shoes were always worn through completely. Nobody could understand how that strange thing happened, because the door remained locked all night. The king made a public announcement to the whole country. He promised that any man who could solve this mystery might choose one daughter to be his wife.

Many young men tried to stay up outside the bedroom door. They always fell fast down because they were very tired. A strong soldier who had been fighting in wars heard about this difficult challenge. He decided to try his luck. On his way to the large house, an old woman gave him a special magic cover. She told him that the cover would make him completely hidden whenever he wore it. When the soldier arrived, the king welcomed him nicely. The king gave him a comfortable room near the daughters. At night, the oldest daughter brought him a cup of sweet wine. The smart soldier did not drink the wine. He pretended to fall fast down instead.

Soon, the twelve daughters got up from bed. They put on lovely clothes and laughed quietly. The youngest daughter whispered that the soldier was sleeping deeply. She said that he would never open his eyes. They opened a secret door under their bed. They walked down into the dark earth. Wearing his magic cover, the soldier followed them closely down the hidden steps. At one point, he touched a daughter dress by mistake. This action made her sister feel quite worried.

Soon they arrived at a wonderful forest. All the leaves in this forest were made of silver and gold. The soldier broke off a small silver branch to keep as physical evidence of their journey. They continued walking until they reached a broad river. Twelve rich men waited in boats by the river. Each daughter got into a boat with one man. They crossed the water to reach a brilliant castle.

Inside the bright castle, beautiful music played while everyone danced happily all night long. The daughters danced until their shoes were completely ruined by morning. Before day, the men brought them back across the river. The daughters ran quickly to their room. The soldier followed them closely. He got back into his bed just before they opened their eyes. When the king questioned him the next morning, the soldier told him everything that he had seen. He showed the king the silver branch and explained the secret ball.

The king called his twelve daughters and asked them if the soldier was telling the truth. Realizing that their secret plan was completely discovered, the daughters admitted everything. The strong soldier chose the oldest daughter to be his lovely wife. He became part of the royal family. From that day on, everyone lived happily together in the great castle. The king was very glad that the mystery was finally solved by the smart soldier. The twelve daughters stopped going to the secret place every night. Their shoes remained clean and new every single morning from then on. Everyone in the country celebrated the wonderful marriage with great joy and music. The king felt very proud because the problem was finally solved. The soldier lived a long life with his wonderful wife. They were happy every day in the royal house.

### C1
There once reigned a monarch whose household was blessed with twelve daughters of surpassing grace, housed together within a single, magnificent chamber. Every evening, as twilight surrendered to shadow, the anxious sovereign secured their heavy oak portal with massive iron bolts before retiring to his own restless slumbers. Yet, with each recurring dawn, an inexplicable phenomenon confounded the entire court, for the princesses' delicate dancing shoes were invariably worn entirely through to the soles. No ingenuity could fathom how this nocturnal transgression transpired, since the heavy door remained obstinately locked from sunset until sunrise. Driven to desperation by the persistent mystery, the king issued a formal proclamation throughout the realm, promising the hand of any daughter to whoever could unravel the secret.

Numerous ambitious youths had already ventured to keep vigil outside the royal bedchamber, only to succumb to an overwhelming and unnatural exhaustion that invariably forced them into slumber. A seasoned veteran of distant campaigns, having heard tales of this formidable trial, resolved to test his fortune against the royal enigma. As he journeyed toward the sprawling palace through a dense and quiet woodland, a frail crone crossed his path and bestowed upon him a singular gift. She presented him with a woven cloak of spun silver, assuring him that its wearer would instantly vanish from mortal sight. Buoyed by this hidden advantage, the soldier arrived at the palace gates, where the grateful monarch received him with exceptional warmth and hospitality.

Quarters were assigned to the weary traveler in a comfortable chamber directly adjacent to the apartments of the twelve princesses. When the evening shadows lengthened, the eldest sister dutifully approached his quarters bearing a silver goblet filled with sweet, spiced wine intended to induce a heavy sleep. The astute soldier, suspecting the draught, discreetly emptied its contents into a sponge concealed within his doublet rather than tasting a single drop. Feigning an immediate and profound collapse, he emitted a theatrical snore and pretended to plunge into an inescapable, dreamless slumber. Not long after, the twelve princesses stirred from their beds with quiet anticipation, shedding their fatigue in favor of elaborate, rustling gowns.

The youngest sister whispered softly that the soldier was breathing heavily and would never unclose his eyes before the morning light. Moving with practiced stealth, they approached their massive wooden bed and pressed a hidden mechanism that released a trapdoor in the stone floor. A subterranean staircase revealed itself, plunging into the deep earth below, and the princesses descended with light, hurried footsteps. Clad in his invisible cloak, the soldier shadowed them closely down the winding steps, taking care to place his boots silently upon each riser. In his haste, he brushed against the heavy velvet skirt of the middle princess, prompting her to voice sudden alarm to her companions.

The eldest sister quickly hushed her, attributing the slight disturbance to a draft whispering through the ancient stonework of the cavernous descent. They pressed onward until they emerged quite suddenly into an enchanted forest of breathtaking and unearthly beauty. Every leaf upon every slender branch was crafted of shimmering silver and gleaming gold, catching a cool, phantom moonlight that illuminated the glade. Determined to secure undeniable proof of their clandestine journey, the soldier reached out and snapped a small silver branch from a nearby bush. The sharp crack echoed faintly through the silent woods, causing the youngest sister to pause and glance backward into the shadows.

Assured that nothing amiss lingered in the gloom, the group continued their march until they reached the edge of a broad and rushing river. Waiting upon the pebble-strewn shore were twelve handsome princes in painted skiffs, each vessel illuminated by a single glowing lantern. Every princess stepped eagerly into a separate boat, pairing with her waiting companion to cross the dark and mirroring water. The observant soldier, moving with quiet swiftness, managed to step aboard the final boat carrying the youngest sister without tipping the craft. The bewildered prince rowing the vessel complained of an unusual weight slowing his progress, unaware of the invisible passenger sharing his seat.

Upon the opposite bank stood a brilliant and towering castle from which drifted the lively strains of invisible violins and golden trumpets. Inside the grand illumination of the ballroom, the couples whirled in frantic, joyous measures that lasted throughout the span of the dark night. The princesses danced with an intoxicating fervor, driven by a secret compulsion that wore their satin shoes completely to shreds by daybreak. Not once did they pause to rest, sustained entirely by the music and the attentive devotion of their aristocratic partners. Only when the first pale fingers of dawn began to paint the eastern sky did the music fade into a breathless silence.

The princes guided their boats back across the dark river, depositing the tired sisters upon the opposite bank with whispered farewells. The princesses ran swiftly up the hidden earthen stairs, their fatigue momentarily forgotten in their eagerness to reach safety. The soldier followed closely behind them, slipping through the secret door just as the first rays of morning struck the palace windowpanes. He returned to his pallet and resumed his posture of deep slumber moments before the eldest sister unlatched the heavy bedroom door. When the king summoned him later that morning to render an account of his vigil, the soldier arose with a steady and confident demeanor.

Standing before the anxious sovereign, the veteran revealed the astonishing truth of the subterranean kingdom and the glittering midnight ball. To substantiate his incredible narrative, he produced the glittering silver branch from his doublet and placed it upon the polished mahogany table. The king, thunderstruck by the tangible evidence, immediately summoned his twelve daughters to the great hall to answer the grave accusation. Confronted with the irrefutable proof and realizing that their elaborate deception had utterly failed, the sisters lowered their heads and confessed everything. The king turned to the triumphant soldier and invited him to claim his reward according to the royal promise made weeks before.

Without a moment of hesitation, the clever veteran chose the eldest daughter to be his cherished bride and partner for the remainder of his days. He was elevated immediately to the inner circle of the royal family, securing a position of high honor within the ancient castle. From that memorable hour onward, the mysterious nightly excursions ceased entirely, and the princesses remained peacefully within their chambers until morning. Their dancing shoes suffered no further damage, remaining pristine and intact with each successive dawn as a quiet testament to a chapter closed. Across the entire realm, joyous bells rang out for weeks to celebrate the magnificent union that had permanently restored peace to the crown.

The sovereign took immense pride in the resolution of a puzzle that had baffled the finest minds in his kingdom for many years. Having proven his remarkable worth, the soldier lived a long and contented life beside his royal wife within the sunlit halls. The twelve sisters found new happiness in their daily lives, content with the familiar rhythms of the court and the warmth of family. No more secrets troubled the heavy oak door, and the palace settled into a tranquil era of lasting harmony and celebration. Thus the strange business of the worn-out shoes passed into legend, remembered only as a curious tale told beside the hearth.

## st-0267 — The Water of Life
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
An old man is very bad. Three young men feel great pain. They want a good life for him. A wise person talk to them. He says water of life help him. The first young man go on a way. He moves on a large big place. He meets a small little man. The first man is very bad. He goes away now. He fall down in a small place. He can not move up. He stays right there. He is not good at all. He looks down every day. The man cannot go back home.

The second young man start his way too. He also meet the small little man. He speaks very bad words to him. He get down between big hard land. The third young man is very kind. He finally leave for the good water. The young man meet the small little man. He gives him food to eat. He use good kind words. The little man tell him about water. He gives him a hand tool. He gives food for the door. The young man open the big house. He finds the water of life. He put water in a small thing. He helps the other two men out.

They travel home together now. The bad father drink the water. He stands up and live well. He is very good every day. The three men are very glad. They look at the water of life. The old man feel very young now. They live in the big house together. Every day is a good day. The family have a fine time. The people look at them. They know the water is good. The old man is well again. He can walk in the place. The young men help him more.

### A2
Once upon a time, an old man was very bad. His three sons felt great love. They loved their father and wanted a fix. A person told them about the water of life. The oldest brother went first on the trip. He went along a high road. He met a small person. The person asked where he was going. The brother answered with bad feeling and went away. Soon, he fell into a pass and could not move.

After the first brother did not return, the second son started. He met the same person on the road. He spoke very badly to him. Because of his bad action, he also became still between rocks. The youngest brother was kind to everyone. He finally left to seek the fix. When he met the person, he gave him food and nice words. The person was pleased by this nice action. He told the boy where the water was. He gave him a strong piece and food pieces.

The young boy opened the building door. He entered the dark building without fear. Inside, he found the clear water and filled a cup. On his way back, he helped his two brothers. The three men traveled home together. They brought the fine fix to their old father. The man drank the water and stood up. He lived happily for many years.

### B1
Once upon a time, an old king became very sick. His three sons felt sad because they loved their father. They wanted to find a health answer for his illness. A good man told them that the water of life could save the king. If anyone drank this special water, he would get well fast. If the sons brought the water, the king would live again. The oldest brother decided to go first on the journey to find the water. He rode his horse along a mountain road. Then, he met a small man. The small man asked him where he was going. The proud brother answered with bad emotion and rode away. Soon, he fell into a narrow mountain pass and could not move forward. He stayed there because he was stuck. He had not listened to the small man. He had not been kind on his journey.

After the first brother did not return, the second son started his own trip. He also met the same small man on the road. He spoke to him very badly. Because of his bad behavior, he also became stuck in a hidden place between the rocks. He could not escape from that place. The youngest brother, who was nice to everyone, finally left to seek the health answer. When the kind young man met the small man, he gave him some food. He used nice words. The small man was pleased by this great behavior. He told him where the special water was hidden. He gave the boy a strong stick. He gave him a pair of food pieces to open the closed house door. The young man followed these instructions carefully. He entered the dark building without fear. He had prepared for the trip.

Inside the house, he found the clear water of life. He filled a small cup with the water. On his way back, he helped his two brothers escape from the mountain pass. The three men traveled home together. They brought the wonderful health answer to their sick father. When the king drank the water, he stood up and lived happily for many years. The family was very happy because the youngest son had saved the king. They had learned a big lesson about being kind to people. The small man had helped them because the young boy was good. Every person in the country celebrated the king getting well again. The king thanked his sons for their hard work. They never forgot that journey. They always helped people who needed food or water. The land was quiet again. The old king smiled every single day.

### B2
Once upon a time, an old king became very sick. His three sons felt great worry and pain in their hearts. They loved their father very much, and they wanted to find a solution for his illness. A wise man told them that the water of life could save the king. If anyone drank this special water, he would get well immediately. The oldest brother decided to go first on the journey to find the water. He rode his horse along a mountain road until he met a small person. The person asked him where he was going. The proud brother answered with anger and rode away. Soon, he fell into a narrow mountain pass and could not move forward. He was stuck between the rocks and could not return home to his family.

After the first brother did not return, the second son started his own trip. He also met the same person on the road and spoke to him very badly. Because of his bad behavior, he also became stuck in a hidden place between the rocks. The youngest brother, who was nice to everyone, finally left to seek the solution. When the good young man met the person, he gave him some food and nice words. The person, who was pleased by this royal behavior, told him where the special water was hidden. He gave the boy a strong stick and a pair of bread pieces to open the ancient castle door. The young royal followed these instructions carefully and entered the dark building without fear. Inside the castle, he found the clear water of life and filled a small cup. On his way back, he helped his two brothers escape from the mountain pass. The three men traveled home together, bringing the wonderful solution to their sick father.

When the king drank the water, he stood up and lived happily for many years. Every person in the country celebrated this event because the king was healthy again. The family remained together for a long time, and peace covered the land. The three brothers learned a valuable lesson about being good to everyone they met. They understood that pride only brought trouble and failure. Therefore, they always treated other people with respect and care. The old king was proud of his youngest son, who had saved his life. Years passed, but the story of the water of life was told again and again across the world. Children grew up listening to this tale, learning that being good always wins in the end. They knew that truth and love would always guide their actions through every difficult day. The country remained safe, and people lived in peace for generations. The memory of the good son stayed in the hearts of all who heard the story.

Long ago, the king had ruled his land with care and wisdom. He always made sure that his people had food and support. When he grew old and weak, his heart filled with worry. He knew that his kingdom needed a strong leader for the future. His three sons were supposed to protect the country after he died. However, the older two sons were unkind and full of pride. They only cared about money and power for themselves. The youngest son was different because he always showed kindness to everyone. He helped people who were poor or sick. When the wise man spoke about the water of life, the king listened closely. He hoped that his sons would learn an important lesson during their travels.

The journey was long and difficult for each brother. Mountains stood tall against the sky, and roads were hard to follow. The oldest brother rode fast because he wanted to find the water first. He did not want to share the glory with anyone else. When he met the small person on the mountain, he shouted at him. The person became angry and used magic to trap him between the rocks. The second brother made the exact same mistake. He laughed at the little person and rode away quickly. Soon, he was also trapped and unable to escape. Both brothers learned that bad behavior leads to terrible problems.

Then the youngest son began his own trip. Unlike his brothers, he stopped to talk to the small person. He shared his bread and offered warm words of friendship. The small person smiled and thanked the kind young man. He told him where the castle was hidden in the dark forest. The young man walked through the trees until he saw the ancient stone walls. He used the stick and the bread pieces to open the heavy door. Inside, the rooms were quiet and dark. He found the water of life in a silver cup. He filled his own bottle and started his journey back home.

On his way out of the castle, he found his brothers stuck in the pass. They looked tired and very sad. The youngest brother did not leave them behind. He used his own strength to help them get out of the rocks. The older brothers felt ashamed of how they had treated him before. They walked together as a family and talked about their mistakes. When they finally reached home, the king was waiting for them. He drank the water and stood up immediately. Everyone in the country cheered and clapped for joy. The king hugged his youngest son and thanked him for saving his life. From that day on, the brothers lived together in peace and happiness.

### C1
Long ago, an aged king ruled his prosperous realm with profound care and steady wisdom, ensuring his people always possessed food and support. When he eventually grew old and weak, a heavy shadow of worry filled his heart, for he knew his kingdom needed a strong leader for the future. His three sons were supposed to protect the country after he departed, yet the older two were unkind and consumed by arrogance. They cared only about accumulating money and seizing personal power, while the youngest son remained remarkably different in spirit. He consistently showed gentle kindness to everyone, readily helping people who were poor or suffering.

Then a terrible sickness befell the sovereign, and deep pain gripped the hearts of the royal brothers who loved their father dearly. A wise counselor informed them that only the legendary water of life could reverse the monarch's fatal illness. Whoever drank this miraculous liquid would recover his health immediately, defying even the grip of death. When the wise man spoke about this cure, the anxious king listened closely, hoping his sons might learn an invaluable lesson during their travels. Driven by a mixture of ambition and duty, the oldest brother decided to embark upon the perilous journey first.

The journey proved long and difficult for each brother, with towering mountains standing against the cold sky and treacherous roads. The oldest son rode his horse rapidly along a winding mountain track, desperate to find the water before anyone else. He had no intention of sharing the glory, marching forward with supreme confidence in his own superiority. Along the way, he encountered a small person who politely asked where he was traveling. The proud brother answered with biting anger, insulted that such a creature dared address him, and spurred his horse away.

Scarcely had he ridden another mile when he found himself trapped in a narrow mountain pass where the jagged rocks closed in around him. Try as he might, he could move neither forward nor backward, completely stuck between the cold stones. He could not return home to his family, paying a heavy price for his unyielding pride. After the first brother failed to return, the second son started his own trip into the wild mountains. He met the exact same small person on the narrow road and spoke to him with cruel disrespect.

Because of his arrogant behavior and sharp tongue, he suffered the exact same fate as his older sibling. He was magically blocked in a hidden, desolate place between the rocks, unable to escape his stony prison. Both arrogant brothers learned through bitter experience that bad behavior invariably leads to terrible problems. They sat in isolation, regretting their foolish choices while the mountains watched in silent judgment. Meanwhile, the youngest brother, who was inherently good-natured toward everyone, finally left to seek the salvation of their father.

Unlike his older brothers, the good young man paused when he met the small person on the mountain path. He dismounted from his horse and greeted the traveler with warmth, willingly sharing his own food and offering kind words of friendship. The small person was deeply pleased by this respectful, noble behavior and decided to aid the youth. He revealed where the special water was hidden inside an ancient, forgotten castle deep within the dark forest. To help him gain entry, the stranger gave the prince a sturdy stick and a pair of fresh bread pieces.

Armed with these unusual items, the young royal followed the precise instructions carefully and entered the dark forest without fear. He walked beneath the towering trees until the ancient stone walls of the castle finally came into view. Using the strong stick to steady his steps and the pieces of bread to appease the giant guardian doors, he opened the heavy entrance. Inside the building, the vast rooms were quiet, shadowy, and filled with an eerie silence. He navigated the empty halls bravely until he discovered the clear water of life gleaming in a silver cup.

Without hesitation, he filled his small bottle with the miraculous liquid and immediately began his journey back home. On his way out of the ancient fortress, he discovered his two older brothers stuck fast in the narrow mountain pass. They looked exhausted, starved, and profoundly ashamed as they stared at the ground. The youngest brother did not abandon them to their fate, choosing mercy over resentment. He used his own strength and the magical stick to help them dislodge the heavy rocks and regain their freedom.

Overwhelmed with gratitude and remorse, the older brothers felt deeply ashamed of how cruelly they had treated him in the past. They walked together down the mountain as a reunited family, talking openly about their past mistakes and foolish pride. When they finally reached home, the anxious king was waiting with dwindling strength for their return. The moment the monarch drank the water of life, he stood up immediately, his vitality restored completely. Every person in the entire country celebrated this joyous event, cheering and clapping in the streets because their beloved ruler was healthy once again.

The king hugged his youngest son tightly and thanked him repeatedly for saving his life. From that day onward, the brothers lived together in peace, harmony, and mutual respect. The three men had learned a profound lesson about treating everyone they met with genuine kindness. They understood fully that arrogance brought only trouble and failure, whereas humility opened doors to success. The old king remained endlessly proud of his youngest son, whose virtuous heart had preserved the kingdom.

Years passed, but the remarkable story of the water of life was told again and again across the entire world. Generations of children grew up listening to this timeless tale, learning that goodness always triumphs in the end. They understood that truth and love would reliably guide their actions through every difficult day they might face. The country remained remarkably safe, and the people flourished in peace for decades to come. The memory of the good son remained forever cherished in the hearts of all who heard the story.

## st-0268 — Chicken Little (Henny Penny)
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Little Little lives in a small house. She lives in a small house. She lives in a small house. She walks under a big plant. Something falls on her head. She looks up at the open top. She looks up at the open top. She looks up at the open top. She says the top is down.

She says the top is down. She runs down the long way. She runs down the long way. She meets Henny Penny. Henny Penny runs with her. Henny Penny runs with her. Henny Penny runs with her. They meet Cocky Locky.

He joins the group now. He joins the group now. He joins the group now. They meet Ducky Lucky and Goosey Loosey. Many other living things run together. Many other living things run together. Many other living things run together. They meet Turkey Lurkey.

Turkey Lurkey asks about the top. Turkey Lurkey asks about the top. Turkey Lurkey asks about the top. Bad friend Foxy comes out. Bad friend Foxy leads them away. They follow him into a hole. They never come out of the room.

### A2
Little animal lived in a small house near the green garden. One fine morning, she walked under a large tree for food. Suddenly, something hit her head hard. She looked up quickly, but she only saw blue light. What a bad day! She felt very much worry. The world is falling down upon the ground. We must run away at once. She left the garden immediately and ran down the road. Soon, she met her good friend Henny Penny.

Where are you going? asked Henny Penny. The world is falling, and I am running to save my life. Really? said Henny Penny. Her eyes opened wide with fear. If the world is falling, I will go with you right now. The two worried animals ran together along the small road. After a short time, they met Cocky Locky. Why are you running so fast? asked Cocky Locky.

The world is falling down, and we are moving away. they told him. Cocky Locky became very worried. He decided to join their group without any pause. The little group ran across the field. They met Ducky Lucky and Goosey Loosey. Every animal heard the same bad story. The world is falling. they cried to everyone. More animals joined the large group.

Many people ran together. Finally, they met Turkey Lurkey near the dark forest. Turkey Lurkey listened to the report. He felt quite unclear. Who told you this? asked Turkey Lurkey. Little animal told us, because something hit her head. answered the others. Turkey Lurkey suggested a place to stay.

If we follow this dog, we will be fine. said Foxy Loxy. He appeared from behind the trees. The animals believed the fine animal completely. They all followed him into his dark room. They never came out again.

### B1
Chicken Little lived in a small house near the green garden. One fine morning, she walked under a large tree to find some food. Suddenly, something hit her soft head hard. She looked up quickly, but she could only see blue sky. That is bad, cried Chicken Little, who was very sad. The sky is falling down upon the ground, and we must run away at once. She left the garden immediately and ran fast down the country road. Soon, she met her good friend Henny Penny. Where are you going in such a rush, asked Henny Penny. The sky is falling, and I am running to save my life, answered Chicken Little. Really, said Henny Penny, whose eyes opened wide with fear. If the sky is falling, then I shall go with you right now. The two sad birds ran together along the path.

After a short time, they met Cocky Locky. Why are you running so fast, asked Cocky Locky. The sky is falling down, and we are escaping from the danger, they cried. Cocky Locky became very worried, and he decided to join their group without delay. As the small group ran across the open field, they met Ducky Lucky and Goosey Loosey. Every animal was told the same terrible story. The sky is falling, they shouted to everyone. More and more birds joined the sad crowd until there were many animals running together. Finally, they met Turkey Lurkey near the edge of the dark forest. Turkey Lurkey listened to the strange report, and he felt quite doubtful. Who told you that this was happening, asked Turkey Lurkey. Chicken Little told us, because the sky hit her head, answered the others.

Turkey Lurkey suggested that they should find a safe place to hide. If we follow this animal, we will be completely safe from the falling sky. A strange animal suddenly appeared from behind the trees. This animal spoke with a calm voice. The simple animals trusted the smart leader completely. They all followed him into his dark home, and they never came out again. Many days passed, but the birds were never seen again by anyone in the country. Chicken Little had lived in the house for many years before this day. She always found good food near the green garden every morning. Henny Penny had walked along the country road many times in the past. Cocky Locky had known Ducky Lucky since they were young animals in the field.

Goosey Loosey had met Turkey Lurkey near the dark forest several times. Turkey Lurkey had never seen such a strange thing happen before this moment. The animal had waited behind the trees for a long time. The animals had trusted him because he seemed very helpful. They had walked into the dark home without any fear at all. Nobody had expected that the journey would end in such a way. The other animals in the country had wondered about the missing birds. They had searched the green garden and the open field for many days. However, the birds had remained inside the dark home during all that time. This story has been told by many parents to their children through the years.

### B2
Chicken Little lived in a small house near the green garden. One fine morning, she walked under a large tree to find some food. Suddenly, something hit her soft head hard. She looked up quickly, but she could only see blue sky. No, cried Chicken Little, who was very frightened. The sky is falling down upon the ground, and we must run away immediately. She left the garden at once and ran fast down the country road. Soon, she met her good friend Henny Penny.

Where are you going in such a rush, asked Henny Penny. The sky is falling, and I am running to save my life, answered Chicken Little. Really, said Henny Penny, whose eyes opened wide with fear. If the sky is falling, then I shall go with you right now. The two frightened birds ran together along the small path. After a short time, they met Cocky Locky. Why are you running so fast, asked Cocky Locky. The sky is falling down, and we are escaping from the danger, they cried.

Cocky Locky became very nervous, and he decided to join their group without delay. As the small group ran across the open field, they met Ducky Lucky and Goosey Loosey. Every animal was told the same terrible story. The sky is falling, they shouted to everyone. More and more birds joined the scared crowd until there were many animals running together. Finally, they met Turkey Lurkey near the edge of the dark forest. Turkey Lurkey listened to the crazy report, and he felt quite doubtful.

Who told you that this was happening, asked Turkey Lurkey. Chicken Little told us, because the sky hit her head, answered the others. Turkey Lurkey suggested that they should find a safe place to hide. If we follow this wild dog, we will be completely safe from the falling sky. This was said by Foxy Loxy, who suddenly appeared from behind the trees. The silly animals trusted the smart leader completely. They all followed him into his dark hole, and they never came out again.

Long ago, the animals lived calm lives in the green land. No one worried about the sky falling down. Chicken Little was simply walking near the garden when the strange event occurred. If she had not walked under that large tree, nothing would have happened to her soft head. Many other birds joined the group as they ran along the road. Each animal believed the frightening story without asking any questions. They ran through the fields and past the forest because they wanted to stay alive. If they had stayed at home, they would have been completely safe.

Foxy Loxy watched the large group of animals approach. He knew that they were silly and easy to trick. The brilliant leader smiled when he saw them running toward him. He promised that his dark hole was a safe place. If the animals had been smarter, they would have run away from him. Instead, they walked right into his trap. The door of the dark hole closed behind them, and they were never seen again. That is why everyone should think carefully before believing strange stories.

Many days passed after the animals disappeared into the dark hole. The green garden remained quiet and empty. Other birds often talked about what had happened near the large tree. If they had known the truth, they would have avoided the same mistake. Life continued in the country, but the story was remembered by everyone. Whenever the wind blew through the trees, people thought about Chicken Little. They learned that listening to crazy reports without proof can lead to great danger. That is why making careful choices is always important in life.

Sometimes people hear news that sounds very frightening. If the news is not true, one should not panic or run away. Instead, people must stop and think about the situation clearly. Asking questions can help save a person from serious trouble. If Chicken Little had asked more questions, she would not have scared all her friends. Instead of running away, she should have looked up at the sky again. Then she would have known that the sky was not falling down at all. Every story needs careful attention before anyone believes it.

The dark forest was a place where danger could hide easily. Animals walked through the trees without knowing the risks. If Foxy Loxy had not been waiting there, the group would have been fine. Bad leaders often try to trick others into following them. If someone offers you a quick way out of trouble, be very careful. Smart people always check the facts before they make a decision. That is the best lesson to learn from the tale of Chicken Little. May everyone remember this story when they hear something strange.

### C1
Chicken Little inhabited a modest, unassuming cottage nestled comfortably near the edge of a remarkably green and flourishing garden. One fine and radiant morning, she ventured beneath the sprawling canopy of a majestic, ancient tree with the sole intention of foraging for some suitable food. Suddenly, an unexpected impact struck her soft head with a sharp and startling force. Instantly, she cast her gaze upward, yet her searching eyes encountered nothing save for the vast, uninterrupted expanse of the serene blue sky. No, cried Chicken Little, whose chest tightened with a sudden and overwhelming terror that threatened to paralyze her limbs. The sky is falling down upon the ground, she whispered to herself in disbelief, and we must run away immediately.

Without a moment's hesitation, she abandoned the sanctuary of the garden and sprinted with desperate urgency down the dusty country road. Before long, she crossed paths with her good friend Henny Penny, who was pecking idly at the gravel. Where are you going in such a rush, inquired Henny Penny, pausing with her head tilted in genuine curiosity. The sky is falling, and I am running to save my life, answered Chicken Little, her voice trembling with unabated panic. Really, said Henny Penny, her eyes widening dramatically with sudden, contagious fear. If the sky is falling, then I shall certainly go with you right now.

The two frightened birds took off together, their wings brushing against one another as they dashed along the narrow, winding path. After a brief yet exhausting stretch, they encountered Cocky Locky strutting near a low stone fence. Why are you running so fast, asked Cocky Locky, observing their frantic pace with mounting bewilderment. The sky is falling down, and we are escaping from the impending danger, they cried in unison, barely breaking their stride. Cocky Locky felt a shiver of anxiety ripple through his feathers, and he resolved to join their desperate group without any delay.

As the small, growing company hurried across the wide-open field, they happened upon Ducky Lucky and Goosey Loosey. Every single animal they encountered along the way was immediately met with the same terrifying report of impending doom. The sky is falling, they shouted loudly to anyone who would listen, gesturing wildly toward the horizon. More and more bewildered birds heard the commotion and chose to join the panicked crowd until a chaotic multitude of animals was rushing together across the meadow. Finally, they reached the very edge of the dark, looming forest, where Turkey Lurkey stood quietly observing the commotion.

Turkey Lurkey listened intently to the hysterical report, his wise old eyes narrowing as he assessed the situation with considerable doubt. Who told you that this catastrophic event was actually happening, asked Turkey Lurkey, his calm demeanor standing in sharp contrast to the surrounding panic. Chicken Little told us directly, because the sky literally fell and hit her head, answered the frantic chorus of surrounding birds. Sensing the overwhelming confusion among the flock, Turkey Lurkey prudently suggested that they find a secure and hidden place before disaster struck. Scarcely had the words left his beak when a sleek, cunning figure emerged from the shadowy undergrowth of the trees.

If we follow this clever guide, we will be completely safe from the falling sky, announced Foxy Loxy, stepping forward with an oily, reassuring smile. This proposal was delivered by the charismatic fox, who had appeared so conveniently from behind the dense foliage to rescue them. The exceedingly gullible animals trusted their self-appointed leader completely, mistaking his flattering words for genuine benevolence. Without pausing to question his motives, they all followed him obediently into the dark, yawning mouth of his underground burrow. Once the last feathered tail had slipped inside, the heavy silence of the forest reclaimed the clearing, and they never emerged again.

Long ago, before that fateful morning, the animals had lived entirely calm and peaceful lives across the green and bountiful land. Not a single creature had ever worried for a moment about the vast heavens collapsing upon their heads. Chicken Little was simply walking near the familiar garden when that strange and deceptive incident occurred. Had she not chosen to wander directly under the heavy branches of that particular tree, nothing would have ever disturbed her soft head. Yet, because she yielded instantly to panic, a tragic chain of events was set irrevocably into motion.

Foxy Loxy had watched the approach of the noisy, panicked procession from his hiding place with calculating amusement. He recognized immediately that they were exceptionally foolish and remarkably easy to manipulate through their own fears. A triumphant grin spread across his face as he watched them rush blindly toward his waiting claws. He had promised them absolute safety within his dark hole, using their own terror as the ultimate tool of persuasion. If any among them had possessed a shred of critical thought, they would have fled from the fox instead of walking willingly into his trap.

Many silent days passed into history after the flock vanished into the subterranean depths of the forest. The green garden remained utterly quiet and empty, devoid of the familiar chirping that once animated the afternoons. Surviving birds in the surrounding countryside frequently discussed the mysterious disappearance near the large tree. Had they possessed the wisdom to seek out the truth, they might have avoided repeating the same fatal errors of judgment. The memory of the event endured as a sobering reminder of the fragility of reason in times of collective hysteria.

Whenever the autumn wind whispered through the branches, the local residents recalled the folly of Chicken Little and her followers. The tale served as a permanent testament to the dangers of accepting hysterical rumors without demanding concrete evidence. Sometimes people hear startling news that sounds profoundly threatening to their daily routines and comfort. If such claims prove entirely groundless, one must resolutely resist the urge to surrender to blind panic and unthinking flight. Instead, stepping back to analyze the situation with a clear and rational mind remains the only proper course of action.

The dark forest always harbored unseen risks for those who wandered through its shaded trails without vigilance. Had Foxy Loxy not been lying in wait that morning, the fleeing flock might have eventually calmed down and dispersed safely. Deceitful leaders frequently exploit moments of widespread anxiety to herd vulnerable populations toward their own destruction. Prudent individuals invariably verify the underlying facts before committing themselves to any sudden or irreversible decisions. That enduring truth constitutes the most vital lesson embedded within the tragic and cautionary tale of Chicken Little.
