## st-0269 — The Three Wishes
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, a man live in a house. The house is small. He lives with a woman. They live near the trees. They work hard every day. Life is very difficult. They have little money for food. One evening, the man walk through the trees.

A person appear before the man. The person give him three thing. Everything he want become true now. The man is very big and happy. He runs back home to the woman. They sit down at the table. The man feel very little food. He looks at the table. He says he want a large food. A long food appear.

The woman become very bad. She says at the man. She says he is not good. She wants the food on his face. The second thing come true. The long food stay on his face. They try to move it off. The food stay on his face. They lose two thing.

The man have one thing left. He thinks about the future. He use his last thing. The food fall back to the table. They have no more money. They are together and they are good. They know an important thing.

### A2
Once upon a time, a poor man lived. He had a small house. The house was near a green place. He lived there with his wife. They worked hard every single day. Life was difficult for them. They had very little money for food. One evening, the man walked through trees. A great thing happened then.

A person appeared before the poor man. The person told him about wishes. Three wishes would be given to him. Whatever he wished for would come true. The man was very surprised. He was also very happy. He ran back home very fast. He wanted to tell his wife.

They sat down at the table. They began to discuss their wishes. The wife wanted to choose with care. The husband felt bad from his walk. They had eaten no good food all week. He wished for a large piece of food.

Right away, a long food item appeared. It was on the table. The wife became very bad. Her husband spent a great chance. He spent it on simple food. She spoke to him with bad feelings. She told him he was wrong. She wished the food item was close.

Right away, the second wish came true. The long food item moved up. It stayed firmly on his face. The couple tried to pull it off. The food item remained there completely. They realized they lost two wishes. They lost them on a simple argument.

Only one wish was left now. The man had to decide. Life would be bad with it. It was on his face all time. The husband used his final wish. He made the food item fall down. It fell back down on the table.

In the end, they had no money. They had no money or high price things. However, they were together. They were also protected. They learned an important thing about life. पैसोंThey learned how good things work.

### B1
Once upon a time, a poor man lived in a small house near the woods. He lived there with his wife. They worked hard every single day of the week. Life was very difficult for both of them. They had very little money for food. One evening, the man was walking through the dark forest. A strange spirit appeared before him. The spirit told him that three wishes would be given. The spirit explained that whatever he wished for would become true.

The man was so surprised. He was so happy. He ran back home as fast as possible. He wanted to tell his wife the good news immediately. When he arrived, they sat down at the table. They began to discuss what they should ask for. The wife wanted to make sure they chose well. However, the man was already feeling very empty from his long walk. Because they had eaten no meat all week, the husband looked at the table. He said that he wished for a large piece of food.

Right now, a long, hot food appeared on the table. The food was a hot meal. The wife became very angry. Her husband had wasted such a great opportunity on a simple item. She shouted at him. She told him that he was not good. She wished the food was stuck to his face. Right away, the second wish came true. The long food jumped up. It attached itself firmly to the man face.

The couple tried to pull it off with their hands. The food remained completely stuck. They realized that they had now lost two of their three valuable wishes. They lost them on a bad argument about food. With only one wish left, the man had to decide. He decided what was most important for their future. If they kept the food on his face for all time, life would be terrible. It would be terrible for both of them. Therefore, the husband used his final wish. He made the food fall back onto the table.

In the end, they had no extra money or gold. They were together and safe. They learned a funny and important lesson. They learned how easily valuable things can be wasted through bad choices. They have lived carefully since that strange evening.

### B2
Once upon a time, a poor man lived in a small house near the woods with his wife. They worked hard every day, but life was difficult and they had very little money for food. One evening, while the man was walking through the forest, a strange event happened to him. A spirit appeared before the poor man and told him that three wishes would be given to him. The spirit explained that whatever he wished for would become true quickly. The man was so surprised and happy that he ran back home quickly to tell his wife the good news. He ran through the dark trees because he wanted to share the amazing story with her. His heart beat fast while he moved along the narrow path. Every single step brought him closer to his small home. He could not wait to see the expression on her face. When he arrived, they sat down at the table and began to discuss what they should ask for. The wife wanted to make sure they chose wisely, but the man was already feeling very empty from his long walk. Because they had eaten no meat all week, the husband looked at the table. He said that he wished for a large piece of food. He dreamed about a great meal that would fill his stomach. His wife listened carefully while he spoke about the wonderful dinner. They both knew that life had been very hard for many months. They hoped that their luck might change tonight. The man repeated his request because he desired something special to eat. He imagined a warm plate sitting right in front of them. Suddenly, a long, hot food item appeared on the table in front of them. The wife became very angry because her husband had wasted such a great opportunity on a simple meal. She shouted at him, telling him that he was silly and that she wished the food was stuck to his nose. Right away, the second wish came true, and the long item jumped up and attached itself firmly to the man's face. The couple tried to pull it off, but the object remained completely stuck. They realized that they had now lost two of their three valuable wishes on a silly argument about food. With only one wish left, the man had to decide what was most important for their future. If they kept the item on his face forever, life would be terrible for both of them. Therefore, the husband used his final wish to make the object fall back onto the table. In the end, they had no extra money or gold, but they were together and safe. They learned a funny and important lesson about how easily valuable things can be wasted through silly choices. The man looked at his wife and smiled because they were still alive. They understood that love was much more significant than any special wish. From that day forward, they lived happily without any further trouble. They went back to their normal work and found rest in their simple life. Whenever they talked about the forest spirit, they laughed together about the strange event. The little house near the woods felt warmer than ever before. They never forgot the lesson that the spirit had brought to their door.

Many people wonder if stories from the past can teach modern lessons. This particular tale shows how small choices may lead to strange results if people do not think carefully. The husband and wife had very little money, yet they survived the difficult times through hard work. Although they made a silly mistake with their first two wishes, they finally understood what mattered most. They were safe together in their small home, which was worth more than gold. Life continued for the couple without any more unusual events in the forest. They stayed in the same village for many years and always remembered the spirit. Whenever friends visited them, the husband told the story of the long item on his nose. Everyone laughed at the funny memory, and the couple felt happy. Such lessons are rarely forgotten by people who live through hard experiences. Rest and love remained in the house until the end of their days. No other spirit ever appeared to them again, but they needed nothing else. Their simple life was full of quiet moments and shared meals. They never wished for anything else because they had each other. The woods outside stayed green through every season of the year. Birds sang in the trees while the wind blew softly across the land. Everything was quiet again, and the world seemed right. They knew that the past could not be changed by any other wish. Both of them walked outside into the garden to look at the trees. The sun shone down upon the ground and made everything look bright. They felt that nothing could ever hurt them again. Each day brought new hope to their hearts. The small house was filled with joy from morning until night. They shared every single meal together with great happiness. The story of the wish became a treasured memory for both of them. Even when times were hard, they remembered how much they loved each other. The simple life was all they ever wanted. They stayed together until the very end of their lives.

### C1
In a modest dwelling on the jagged perimeter of the deep woods, a destitute woodcutter and his devoted wife eked out a meager existence. Though they labored from dawn until dusk, the relentless weight of poverty pressed down upon them, leaving little coin for the barest necessities of life. One crisp evening, as the husband traversed the darkening timberlands, a peculiar phenomenon disrupted the mundane rhythm of his solitary walk. A luminous spirit materialized directly in his path, radiating an ethereal glow that cast long, dancing shadows across the gnarled roots of the ancient oaks. The supernatural visitor declared that three unconditional wishes would be granted to the humble man, promising that whatever he desired would instantly materialize.

Overwhelmed by a tumultuous mixture of disbelief and euphoria, the man abandoned his usual measured pace and bolted toward his small home. He sprinted through the gathering gloom, his boots crunching against the frost-kissed foliage while the cool night air burned his lungs. His heart hammered against his ribs in a breathless cadence, each rapid stride propelling him closer to the woman who shared his heavy burden. He could scarcely wait to behold the expression of astonishment take hold of her features when he delivered the miraculous news. Upon bursting through the wooden door, he found his wife tending the hearth, and they immediately sat down at the rough-hewn table to deliberate their immense fortune.

While the prudent wife urged caution, advising that they weigh every prospective option with utmost care, the husband felt an overwhelming emptiness gnawing at his stomach. Having tasted no meat throughout the grueling course of the entire week, his gaze drifted longingly toward the center of the barren table. He vocalized a sudden, unrefined craving, declaring that he wished for a magnificent, oversized piece of sausage to satisfy his hollow belly. His wife listened with a mixture of exasperation and disappointment as he rhapsodized about the savory meal that would banish their persistent hunger. They both acutely remembered the endless months of deprivation, secretly hoping that tonight might finally mark the turning of their desperate luck.

Driven by a powerful desire for immediate comfort, the man repeated his impulsive request, picturing a steaming plate sitting enticingly before them. No sooner had the words escaped his lips than a loud thud echoed through the cramped room, followed by the sudden appearance of a long, steaming sausage. The object rested heavily on the wooden surface, exhaling a rich, aromatic steam that filled the drafty kitchen with an intoxicating scent. Instead of feeling grateful, the wife flared with sudden rage, bitterly condemning her husband for squandering such a monumental opportunity on a vulgar snack. She screamed that he was an absolute fool, wishing in her white-hot anger that the greasy food item would attach itself firmly to his face.

Instantly, the malicious second wish manifested into reality with a sharp snap, and the lengthy object launched itself upward from the table. It struck the center of the husband's face with undeniable force, embedding itself securely against his skin directly beneath his bewildered eyes. The terrified couple lunged forward, their frantic fingers clawing at the grease-slicked casing in a desperate attempt to dislodge the grotesque appendage. Despite their combined strength and frantic pulling, the foreign object remained as immovably anchored as if it had been forged into his very bone. A cold realization washed over them as they stared at one another, recognizing that two of their three miraculous wishes had evaporated over a petty quarrel.

With only a single chance remaining to alter their destiny, the husband weighed the grim reality of his permanently disfigured future. If the cumbersome item remained affixed to his visage indefinitely, their daily interactions would become a humiliating ordeal of endless public ridicule. The sheer absurdity of the predicament warred with the stark terror of living out their remaining days as spectacles of community mockery. Recognizing that vanity was entirely worthless compared to basic human dignity, the husband prepared to utter the final, corrective command. He focused his remaining willpower on the offending piece of meat, directing his last supernatural authority toward a single, humble outcome.

With a breathless sigh of resignation, the husband used his final wish to command the stubborn object to detach and drop back onto the wooden table. The heavy sausage slipped effortlessly from his skin, tumbling downward with a dull thud to rest once more amid the quiet shadows of the kitchen. They stood in profound silence, surveying the empty room that still held no extra gold, no fine silks, and no promises of permanent wealth. Yet, as the initial shock subsided, a profound sense of relief washed over them, replacing the bitter tension that had previously poisoned their home. They were safely together, breathing the same air, and miraculously bonded by the bizarre ordeal they had barely managed to survive.

Looking across the narrow table, the husband caught his wife's eye, and a gentle, understanding smile broke through their shared exhaustion. They laughed softly at the sheer folly of their actions, realizing how easily human greed and impatience could sabotage the truest blessings. The little house near the woods felt palpably warmer than it had in years, radiating a quiet peace that transcended material wealth. They understood with absolute clarity that genuine companionship and mutual devotion outweighed any fantastical wish the forest spirit could ever bestow. From that evening onward, they returned to their arduous daily labor, finding a deep, restorative contentment in the simplicity of their shared existence.

Whenever curious neighbors or visiting friends brought up the legendary woodland spirit, the couple merely exchanged knowing smiles and shared the humorous tale. The absurd image of the long sausage protruding from the husband's nose became a cherished family joke that never failed to provoke genuine laughter. Hardship still visited their door from time to time, but it no longer possessed the power to shatter their internal harmony or breed bitter resentment. They tended their modest garden through the changing seasons, watching the green canopy of the nearby woods rustle gently in the afternoon breeze. No further magical entities ever materialized in their clearing, but neither of them harbored the slightest desire for supernatural intervention.

Time flowed onward like a steady river, carrying them quietly into their golden years without further disruption to their peaceful routine. The sun continued to illuminate their small garden every morning, casting a golden hue over the soil where they cultivated their meager crops. They broke every single piece of daily bread together, savoring the quiet moments of companionship that defined the true wealth of their lives. Even when winter winds howled fiercely against the windowpanes, the interior of their rustic cottage remained a sanctuary of warmth and affection. They remained steadfastly by each other's side until the very end of their days, needing nothing more than the love they had so nearly lost.

## st-0270 — The Ant and the Dove
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One small little insect live near water. The day is very big. The light is strong. The little insect feel no water in him. He walks to the water. The water move is very strong. The little insect fall in. The water carry him fast. He can go down. He can go down now. He can go down in water.

A white animal watch him. She feels much love. The white animal send one little thing. The little thing is small. The little insect come on it. The little thing reach land. The little insect is good and no problem. He is back on the land.

Days pass by. The little insect walk in little land. He sees one bad man. The man have a big thing. He wants the white animal. The man look at the animal. He wants to end her life.

The little insect remember the animal. He must help her now. The little insect move close to the man. He cut the man hard. The man feel bad problem. The man feel hard problem in hand.

The man call out large. His thing move and go. The big sound pass the animal. The large thing make the animal no good. The animal go away now. She is good and no problem.

### A2
Once, a little animal lived near deep water. The summer day was very hot. The small animal felt bad and wanted drink. He walked down to the water to drink. The current of the water was strong. The poor animal fell into the big water. The water carried the small animal away fast. He tried to move his arms. The problem was very serious. He could easily go under the water.

High up in a green tree, a white animal watched. The white animal felt great care for him. The white animal broke a small green plant from a plant part. She dropped the plant into the water. The animal got onto the plant. He stayed well. The plant reached the side. The animal stepped onto land.

Several days later, the small animal walked through the green ground. Suddenly, he saw a man with a heavy thing. The bad man wanted to catch the white animal. The white animal rested in the tree. The man aimed his thing to use against the white animal. The little animal remembered the water problem. He knew that he must help his good friend.

The good animal came close to the foot. Then, the little animal bit the man hard. The man felt a hard body pain. He spoke out with a high voice. His hands moved. The hit missed the white animal. The big sound made the white animal full of fear. It flew away into the blue air. The white animal was saved by the small animal.

### B1
Once upon a time, a small animal lived near a deep river. The summer day was very hot. The little worker felt extremely dry. He walked down to the water so that he could drink. However, the current was very strong. The poor person fell directly into the cold river. The water began to carry the tiny animal away very fast. He tried to swim, but the danger was serious. He could fall down and lose his life in the deep water. High up in a green tree, a white bird watched the whole situation. The bird felt great love for the little worker who was in such trouble.

Without any delay, the bird broke a small green part from the tree branch. She dropped the piece of plant right into the water near the animal. The smart worker climbed onto the plant quickly and stayed safe. Soon, the plant reached the edge, and the animal stepped onto dry ground. He has survived the terrible accident because the bird has helped him. Several days later, the animal was walking through the green field. Suddenly, he saw a man who carried a heavy weapon. The bad hunter wanted to catch the white bird. The bird was resting in the tree. The man aimed his gun carefully and prepared to shoot the bird.

The little worker remembered how the bird had saved his life during the river accident. He knew that he must help his good friend immediately. Without making any noise, the worker moved close to the hunter foot. Then, the animal bit the man as hard as he could. The hunter felt a sharp pain and shouted in surprise. His gun shook, and the shot missed the white bird completely. The sound made the bird feel afraid. She flew away into the blue sky without any injury. Thus, the bird was saved by the tiny animal whom she had helped before.

### B2
Once upon a time, a small animal lived near a deep stream. The summer day was very hot, and the tiny creature felt extremely dry. He walked down to the water so that he could drink some cool liquid. However, the current was very strong, and the poor animal fell directly into the big river. The water began to carry the tiny creature away very fast. He struggled to swim, but the danger was serious because he could easily sink.

High up in a green tree, a white bird watched the whole situation. The bird felt great sadness for the little creature who was in such trouble. Without any delay, the bird broke a small green plant part from the tree branch. She dropped the piece of plant right into the water near the sinking animal. The smart creature climbed onto the plant part quickly and stayed safe. Soon, the floating plant reached the edge of the river, and the animal stepped onto dry ground. The forest was quiet, and the sun shone brightly above the green trees as the bird flew away. Both friends felt happy because they were safe from any further danger today.

Several days later, the animal was walking quietly through the green grass. Suddenly, he saw a man who carried a heavy gun. The bad person wanted to catch the white bird, which was resting quietly in the tree above. The man aimed his weapon carefully and prepared to shoot the gentle bird. The little animal remembered how the bird had saved his life during the river accident. He knew that he must help his good friend immediately.

Without making any noise, the strong creature walked close to the shooter foot. Then, the animal bit the man as hard as he possibly could. The shooter felt a sharp pain and shouted loudly in surprise. His gun shook, and the shot missed the white bird completely. The loud noise frightened the bird, which flew away into the blue sky without any injury. Thus, the bird was saved by the tiny animal whom she had helped before.

The sun shone brightly above the green trees as the bird flew away. Both friends felt happy because they were safe from any further danger today. The little animal walked back to his home in the deep forest with a glad heart. He knew that helping others always brings good results in the end. The bird continued her journey across the bright blue sky until she reached her own home. Every single moment of that warm summer day taught them a valuable lesson about friendship and care. They understood that small acts of care can change lives in surprising ways.

Many seasons passed by, and the two friends never forgot that extraordinary afternoon. Whenever they met near the stream, they shared a quiet moment of happiness. The water flowed steadily past the green banks while the wind whispered softly through the leaves. Life in the forest continued without any trouble, and peace remained over the whole area. Sometimes, the little animal would look up at the tall trees and remember his rescue. He always felt a deep sense of appreciation toward the white bird who had saved him.

The bird, on her part, felt proud of the small creature for his quick action against the shooter. Such stories are passed down through generations of forest people as an example of true friendship. Everyone in the woods knew that helping someone in need is always the right thing to do. The world is full of challenges, but friends can overcome any difficulty if they stand together. Therefore, people and other groups should always treat one another with care and respect.

As the evening approached, the sky turned a lovely shade of orange and pink. The stars began to appear one by one above the quiet river. Night fell gently upon the earth, bringing rest and comfort to all living creatures. The little animal closed his eyes and moved into a deep sleep inside his safe home. The white bird rested her head beneath her wing high up in the secure branches. All was quiet and calm in the wonderful forest where they lived.

### C1
Beneath the dappled shadows of an ancient forest, a small ant made his home near the banks of a deep and restless stream. The summer air hung heavy with heat, baking the damp earth and leaving the tiny creature parched with a desperate thirst. Drawn by the murmur of the current, he descended the mossy slope to find some cool water. However, the afternoon river ran fierce and swift, swollen by recent rains higher in the hills. As he leaned down to drink, the unstable bank crumbled beneath his delicate feet, plunging him directly into the churning rapids.

The icy water swallowed him instantly, dragging the helpless insect toward the center of the roaring channel. He thrashed with all his might, his thin legs beating against the relentless weight of the water. Every breath felt like his last as the current tossed him like a fallen leaf. High above the chaos, perched upon the emerald canopy of a towering oak, a white dove surveyed the scene with wide, gentle eyes. A profound pity stirred within her breast as she watched the tiny creature fighting a losing battle against the tide.

Without a moment to lose, the dove snapped a tender green twig from the branch beside her. She fluttered downward, dropping the slender vegetation precisely into the water near the exhausted ant. Clinging to the floating leaf with desperate hope, the ant hauled his shivering body onto the makeshift raft. Safely buoyed by the buoyant stem, he drifted toward the shallow edge where the current finally relented. He crawled ashore onto the warm mud, trembling from exhaustion but intensely grateful for his miraculous survival.

The forest around them grew quiet once more, bathed in the golden slant of late afternoon sunlight as the dove drifted back into the sky. Both creatures felt a quiet relief settle over their spirits, knowing that fate had spared them a tragedy on this quiet day. Several days later, the ant was foraging through the tall grass, his mind drifting peacefully as he navigated the green wilderness. Suddenly, a sharp glint of metal caught his attention through the stalks. He froze, peering ahead to discover a local hunter standing motionless with a heavy gun raised to his shoulder.

The man was tracking a familiar silhouette resting among the higher branches of a nearby tree. It was the very same white dove who had delivered the leaf during his perilous hour of need. The hunter narrowed his eyes, adjusting the barrel as he prepared to pull the trigger on the unsuspecting bird. Instantly, the ant recalled the rushing water, the suffocating cold, and the sudden mercy that had saved his life. He knew with absolute certainty that he could not stand by while his benefactor faced imminent destruction.

Moving with frantic speed, the ant darted across the roots and pine needles until he reached the hunter's boot. He climbed the thick leather without hesitation, his mandibles quivering with nervous determination. Finding bare skin exposed above the woolen sock, the ant sank his sharp jaws into the man's ankle with every ounce of his strength. A sharp cry of surprise ripped from the hunter's throat as a sudden wave of pain shot up his leg. His hands jerked violently, causing the heavy weapon to discharge harmlessly into the empty air.

The deafening crack of the gunshot shattered the peaceful afternoon silence, echoing across the valley. Startled by the explosion, the white dove snapped her wings open and soared upward into the safety of the blue sky. She glanced down just long enough to see the man nursing his bleeding ankle and the tiny ant slipping away into the undergrowth. Though she did not know the exact mechanics of her salvation, she understood that her previous kindness had somehow returned to her. The forest absorbed the noise quickly, returning to its usual rhythm as the hunter gathered his belongings and limped away in frustration.

The ant walked back toward his subterranean home with a light heart and a profound sense of fulfillment. He reflected on the strange balance of the world, realizing that compassion is never wasted. Meanwhile, the dove continued her graceful flight across the vast expanse of the changing sky until she reached her roost. That single warm afternoon had woven their separate lives together into an enduring narrative of mutual care. They had learned that even the most insignificant beings possess the power to alter the course of destiny.

As the seasons shifted over the ensuing months, neither the ant nor the dove ever forgot the events of that extraordinary day. Whenever their paths crossed near the meandering stream, they would pause to acknowledge one another with a silent, knowing grace. The water continued its steady journey past the green banks, while the wind murmured softly through the whispering leaves. Peace reigned supreme across the woodland canopy, undisturbed by the harsh realities of the wider world beyond. The ant would often gaze upward into the branches, whispering a silent expression of gratitude to his feathered protector.

For her part, the dove cherished the memory of the brave insect who had defended her against such insurmountable odds. Tales of their unusual bond eventually spread among the forest dwellers, becoming a cherished parable of loyalty. Every resident in the woods understood that true strength lies not in physical size, but in the willingness to protect others. Challenges would always arise in the wild, but unity and kindness remained the ultimate shields against adversity. They lived out their days knowing that respect and empathy are the foundational pillars of any thriving community.

As evening approached, the sky dissolved into magnificent gradients of burning orange and bruised purple. The first hesitant stars pierced the darkening velvet above the meandering river. Night descended with gentle footsteps, wrapping the forest in a blanket of cool, restorative slumber. The little ant closed his eyes deep within his earthen chamber, breathing slowly in total security. High above, the white dove tucked her head beneath her wing, resting peacefully within the cradle of the secure branches.

### B2

When they finally found a dry place under a big tree, they decided to stop for the night. They sat down on the soft grass and shared their remaining food. The wind blew softly through the leaves above their heads. The man closed his eyes and thought about all the lessons he had learned that day. He knew that his old ideas about power were not complete. The animal went into deep rest quickly after a long day of walking. The quiet night surrounded them like a warm cover. If anyone had seen them resting together, that person would have been very surprised. After all, a man and a wild animal do not usually travel as partners. However, their shared journey had created a special bond between them. The morning sun would soon rise to start a brand new day of their long adventure. They were ready to face whatever came next on the road.

## st-0272 — The Peddler of Swaffham
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
John is a good man. He lives in Swaffham. He works hard every day. He walks to make money for family. His family is small. John likes to walk. He walks to many places. He is a worker. He helps people. He is a good man. He has a little house.

One night, John has a rest. A person tells him about London. A great thing is there. John thinks about that rest. He wants that thing. He wants to go far. He wants to see London. He stays in his house. He thinks more and more. He wants the thing very much.

John leaves home. He walks to London. London is big. John looks for the thing on the way. Nothing happens all day. The sun goes down. John feels bad. A shop owner asks him. He asks why he is there. John tells the man about his rest. The shop owner looks at John.

The shop owner says no. He says such rests mean nothing. The shop owner has a rest too. He sees a house in Swaffham. A worker has gold under his bed. John knows that house is his own home. John thinks about his house. He knows the place very well.

John thanks the man. He runs back to Swaffham. He goes into his room. He works under his bed. He finds gold and silver. His family is happy now. John is happy too. He stays in his home. He does not walk far again. He has a good life now.

### A2
Once upon a time, a simple man lived in Swaffham. His name was John. He worked hard as a seller of small things. He walked from house to house every single day. He tried to sell small things to people. John did this every morning and every afternoon. He walked along many streets to find customers. People sometimes bought small items from him. John was always very hard at work. He lived alone in a small house. His life was simple and low in sound. He wanted to earn enough money for food. One night, John had a very bad dream. A voice told him to go to London town. The voice said that a great thing would be found there. The voice spoke very clearly to him. John thought about the dream again and again. He wondered if the dream could be real.

Finally, the seller decided to leave his home. He walked all the way to London. The big city was full of many people. When John arrived in the city, he found many shops. He spent a whole day watching for the money. Nothing happened during the afternoon. The light began to go down. John felt very bad, and his feet were bad. He sat near a store to rest. The store owner came outside to ask him why. John told the story about his dream. He explained the voice and the great prize. The store owner began to laugh out big. He told John that bad dreams should not be followed. The store owner said he had dreamed of Swaffham. He dreamed a seller named John had a closed prize. The prize was under his bed. John knew immediately that the house was his own home.

The happy seller thanked the store owner. He turned back toward his town as fast as he could. John arrived back at his house. He began to work under his bed with his hands. Deep in the ground, he found a box of money. John was very happy with his new life. He never forgot the voice from the dream. He stayed in his home town for the rest of his days.

### B1
Once upon a time, a simple man lived in a town named Swaffham. His name was John. He worked hard as a person who sold things from house to house. He walked from house to house every day to sell items to people. One night, John had a strange dream while he was sleeping in his bed. A voice told him that a great reward was waiting on a bridge in London. The dream came back to him again and again during many nights. If he goes to the city, he will find a great gift. Finally, the man decided to walk all the way to London. When John arrived at the bridge, he found shops and heavy traffic everywhere. He spent a whole day watching for the promised reward near the water. Nothing happened, and the sun began to go down behind the houses. John felt very tired, and his feet hurt from the long journey. As he rested near a small store, the owner of the store asked him why he was there.

John told the truth about his strange dream to the store owner. The store owner began to laugh out with a big sound. The man told John that bad dreams should never be followed by anyone. The store owner said that he had dreamed of a house in Swaffham too. He said a man named John had gold under his bed in that house. John knew immediately that the house was his own home in the town. He realized that if he checks his bedroom, he will see the money. The happy man thanked the store owner and turned back to his town. When John arrived at his house, he began to look under his bed. Deep in the ground, a massive box of gold was discovered by him. He has always wanted a better life, and now he has it. He has found the gold, and he is very rich today. John will stay in his house and enjoy his life with all the new gold. He will never forget the strange dream that led him to such wealth. The people in the town were very happy for him after that day. John always tells his story to friends who visit his home in Swaffham.

### B2
Once upon a time, a simple man lived in a small town named Swaffham. His name was John, and he worked hard as a traveler to make money for his family. Although his life was difficult, John tried to keep a good heart. He walked from house to house every single day, trying to sell small things to people. He wanted to provide a good life for everyone in his home, but money was always tight. Sometimes he felt very tired, yet he never stopped walking because his family needed food. Every evening he returned with very little coin, but he always smiled at his children. His wife would look at him with love, knowing how hard he worked during those long hours. They hoped that one day their situation would improve, bringing happiness and rest. Life in the town was quiet, and most people knew each other quite well. John was known as an honest person who always kept his word to his friends. No one had any bad thing to say about him, because he was always kind. However, his pockets remained mostly empty, and winter was coming soon. The cold weather would make travel much harder, increasing their daily worries. John often sat by the fire, wondering how they would survive the coming months. He prayed for some good change to change their life and save them from poverty. If he had more money, they would not worry about the cold. If the winter were shorter, life would be much easier for everyone in the house.

One night, John had a very strange dream while he was sleeping in his bed. In his dream, a voice told him that if he went to London bridge, a great fortune would be found there. The voice spoke clearly, and the image stayed in his mind for many days. At first, John thought the dream meant nothing, but it kept coming back to him again and again. He tried to ignore the thought, but it grew stronger inside his head each morning. Could the dream be real, or was it just another trick of his tired mind? He discussed the matter with his wife, who listened carefully to every word. She suggested that perhaps he should make the journey to see what might happen. After much thought, the traveler decided to leave his home and walk all the way to London. It took him several days to reach the big city, which was full of noise and strange people. When John arrived at London bridge, he found that it was covered with shops and crowded with traffic. He spent a whole day walking across the bridge, waiting and watching for the wealth that the voice had promised. Nothing happened during the afternoon, and the sun began to go down. John felt very tired, and his feet hurt from so much walking. If he had stayed at home, his feet would not hurt so much. If the bridge were empty, he would find the gold faster.

As he sat near a market to rest, the store owner came outside and asked him why he was there. The friendly manager looked closely at the tired traveler and wanted to know his story. John decided to tell the truth about his strange dream. He explained how a voice had told him to come to London bridge for a great fortune. When the store owner heard this, he began to laugh out loud. The man shook his head and told John that silly dreams should never be followed. The man said that he himself had dreamed of a house in Swaffham. In that dream, a traveler named John had a hidden gold box under his bed. He laughed again, saying that if he were silly like John, he would spend his time digging up every garden. When John heard those words, his heart gave a big jump. He knew immediately that the house the man spoke of was his own home. Without wasting a single minute, the happy traveler thanked the man and turned back toward his town. If the store owner had not spoken, John would never have known the truth. If he had not traveled to London, the secret would have remained hidden.

He walked home as fast as he could, feeling full of energy and hope. When John finally arrived back at his house, he took some tools and began to dig under his bed. Deep in the ground, a massive box of gold and silver coins was discovered at last. The discovery brought a wonderful new life to his family. They were finally rich, and all their money problems disappeared completely. John bought food, clothes, and a better house for his family. They never had to worry about the cold winter or lack of food again. The strange dream had changed everything, proving that sometimes good things really do happen. People in the town talked about the amazing event for many years later. John lived a long, happy life, always remembering the voice that sent him to London. If they had been poor forever, they would not have bought a new house. If John had ignored the dream, the gold would still be under the floor.

### C1
In the quiet market town of Swaffham, where the mist clung stubbornly to the damp thatch of the roofs, there lived a humble peddler named John. Day after day, driven by an unyielding sense of duty, he walked the winding country lanes to earn a meager living for his household. Though his physical frame was constantly fatigued by the endless miles, he maintained a gentle spirit that endeared him to his neighbors. He carried a pack filled with small trinkets and household necessities, hoping against hope that his labor might yield enough coin to secure their daily bread. His devoted wife met him each evening with a tender look, fully aware of the toll those grueling hours took upon his weary limbs.

Poverty cast a long, cold shadow over their modest hearth, and the approaching winter promised trials far harsher than they had yet endured. Had they possessed even a small reserve of wealth, the impending frost would not have filled their minds with such persistent dread. John often sat by the dying embers of the fire, pondering how they would survive the bleak months ahead without falling deeper into debt. He was known throughout the parish as a man of absolute integrity, whose word was never broken and whose kindness never wavered. Yet honesty alone could not purchase firewood, nor could his spotless reputation put food upon the bare wooden table for his children.

One restless night, as sleep finally overtook his exhausted mind, John experienced a remarkably vivid and unusual dream. A disembodied voice echoed through the vast emptiness of his slumber, declaring with absolute certainty that a great fortune awaited him at London Bridge. Not only did the pronouncement ring with startling clarity, but the image of the distant bridge embedded itself deeply within his consciousness. When morning arrived, he initially dismissed the vision as a mere phantom born of exhaustion and nightly anxiety. Day after day, however, the mysterious command returned to haunt his thoughts, growing increasingly difficult to ignore.

Could the nocturnal whisper possibly hold a kernel of truth, or was it merely another cruel trick played by a desperate imagination? Unable to bear the uncertainty any longer, he confided the entire strange occurrence to his wife, watching her face for any sign of doubt. She listened intently to every syllable, her practical mind weighing the slim possibilities against the crushing reality of their destitute state. It was she who finally suggested that he undertake the long journey southward to discover what fate might have in store for him. After much anxious deliberation, the traveler resolved to leave his familiar surroundings and walk all the way to the sprawling capital.

It took him several grueling days of steady travel to reach the outskirts of London, a city that overwhelmed his rustic senses with its deafening roar. When John finally stepped onto London Bridge, he found himself entirely unprepared for the chaotic spectacle unfolding before his eyes. The ancient stone structure was packed tightly with small shops and blocked by a relentless stream of carts, merchants, and pedestrians. For hours he wandered back and forth across the crowded span, watching anxiously for any sign of the promised wealth. Had the bridge been mercifully empty, or had he arrived at a quieter hour, his task might have proved far less daunting.

As the afternoon waned and shadows lengthened across the river Thames, John sought refuge near a bustling market stall to ease his aching feet. Had he chosen to remain safely at home in Swaffham, his weary joints would not have throbbed with such sharp, unrelenting pain. The proprietor of the shop, a sharp-eyed yet fundamentally kind-hearted merchant, noticed the traveler slumped against a wooden post. Intrigued by the man's profound exhaustion and forlorn expression, the store owner approached him and gently inquired about the purpose of his visit. Hesitant at first, John eventually decided to confide the whole truth, recounting the peculiar dream that had driven him hundreds of miles from home.

Scarcely had the peddler finished describing the mysterious voice than the merchant burst into a loud, hearty fit of laughter. Shaking his head at the naive simplicity of the traveler, the store manager advised him never to place any faith in foolish nighttime visions. The merchant declared that he himself had recently suffered through a remarkably persistent dream concerning a remote house in distant Swaffham. In that nocturnal fantasy, according to the shopkeeper, a humble peddler named John possessed a massive chest of gold buried beneath his own bed. The store owner chuckled, adding that if he were gullible enough to chase such nonsense, he would spend every waking moment digging up random gardens.

Upon hearing those extraordinary words, John felt his heart leap violently against his ribs as a sudden revelation struck him with blinding force. He instantly recognized the precise dwelling the merchant described as his own modest cottage back in Swaffham. Had the friendly store owner kept his thoughts to himself, the secret would have remained forever buried beneath the floorboards. Without wasting a single precious moment, the overjoyed traveler thanked the merchant profusely and turned his footsteps back toward the north. Had he not undertaken the arduous pilgrimage to London, the golden hoard would have slumbered in darkness for the rest of his natural days.

Fueled by a sudden surge of adrenaline and boundless hope, he traversed the long road homeward with a lightness in his step he had not felt in years. When John finally crossed the threshold of his familiar cottage, he immediately gathered the necessary tools to begin his clandestine excavation. Beneath the sleeping quarters, deep in the dark earth, his shovel struck something hard and metallic with a dull, echoing thud. With trembling hands, he uncovered a massive chest overflowing with heavy gold and silver coins that glittered softly in the dim light. The staggering discovery swept away the dark cloud of poverty that had hung over his family for generations.

Instantly, their precarious existence was transformed into one of comfort, security, and quiet abundance. John purchased wholesome food, fine garments, and a much sturdier house where his children could grow without the constant terror of the winter cold. Had they remained destitute forever, they never would have known the sweet relief of financial peace and proper shelter. For decades afterward, townsfolk whispered in awe about the miraculous chain of events that had brought sudden wealth to the honest peddler. John lived out his remaining years in serene contentment, frequently marveling at the mysterious voice that had directed him all the way to London.

---
